NOT checked by eye (button look, text size change, gutter width).
- Docs (viewer): `opusdm-viewer` did not open `.webp` pictures (`Couldn't recognize the image file format`): pictures go through `gdk_pixbuf::Pixbuf::from_file` and the system had no WebP gdk-pixbuf loader. No code change: the fix is the `webp-pixbuf-loader` system package, now documented in section 3.28 of `docs/usage.md` (same loader serves Lister preview, Icon View thumbnails, background picker).
- Feature (viewer, ipc): `opusdm-viewer` shows source code and plain texts syntax coloured. New `MediaKind::Source` (`SOURCE_EXTS`, 85 extensions from `rs` to `txt` / `log`, and `SOURCE_NAMES`: `Makefile`, `GNUmakefile`, `Dockerfile`, `Containerfile`, `justfile`, `PKGBUILD`); a file opened with an unknown extension is shown as source when it starts with `#!` or its first 4 KiB are UTF-8 without NUL (`looks_like_text`), else tried as a picture as before. The language comes from the shebang first, then name, extension, first line (`highlight::syntax_for`). New `source_view.rs`: read-only monospace `TextView` without wrapping; the text shows at once and a thread colours it 500 lines at a time (`highlight_in_batches`), the spans are applied on the main loop as they arrive and a generation counter stops the thread of a file no longer on screen. New `line_numbers.rs`: a `gtk::Widget` subclass set as the left gutter of the view that draws the numbers of the lines on screen from the view's own line positions (a second text view of numbers drifted away from the text in a 200 000 line file, because each view estimates the heights of lines not yet laid out); **l** hides / shows it (`Action::LineNumbers`). Search, Dark switch and focus go through the `TextDoc` trait for both texts (`State::_text_doc`, `_show_text`, `_text_search`). The search now scrolls to the match without moving a long line sideways when the match fits in the page (`xalign` from the match position, Markdown too). Folders bring source texts into the gallery; the strip shows the extension (`RS`) or `TEXT`; the info bar shows `Rust  -  120 lines`. New default file type **Source file** (only for a new `filetypes.json`). Tests: 3 new in `kind.rs`, 1 in `keys.rs`, 1 in `strip.rs`, `gallery.rs` and `filetypes.rs` updated (a `.txt` is now a Source file). Checked under Xephyr with screenshots: a 200 280 line / 8 MB Rust file shows coloured at the top after about 1 s and stays readable while the rest is coloured, numbers aligned at line 172 000 and at the end, Ctrl+End reaches the last line after a few seconds (GTK lays the lines out in the background); a `#!/usr/bin/env python3` script without extension is coloured as Python; Markdown code blocks are coloured; search scrolls the match a third down the page, and on a 330 character line shows the match at the far right; **l** hides the numbers; no GTK warning. NOT checked by eye: the light theme colours.
- Feature (viewer): Markdown code blocks with a known language (` ```rust `, ` ```py `, ` ```sh {.x} `: the first word of the info string) are syntax coloured with the new `syntect` dependency (no default features: `default-syntaxes` + `regex-fancy`, pure Rust). New `highlight.rs` (no GTK) puts each token in one of 14 categories (`Category`: comment, string, escape, number, constant, keyword, operator, type, function, macro, tag, attribute, heading, invalid) by scope selectors, so a view has one `syn-*` tag per category and dark / light only changes the tag colours (One Dark like and GitHub like palettes). Also in it: `syntax_for` (shebang first, then file name, extension, first line), `highlight_in_batches` (spans in characters, handed out every N lines, stops at a line over 16 KiB) and `pieces`. New `text_doc.rs`: the search marks (`TextSearch`), the `TextDoc` trait and the dark / light helper, moved out of `markdown_view.rs` to be shared with the coming source view. 9 tests in `highlight.rs`, 3 new in `markdown.rs`.
- Feature (viewer): a **Dark** switch in the bar, next to the search field and for Markdown only, turns the document dark or light without touching the window (`MarkdownView::set_dark`, CSS classes `viewer-doc-dark` / `viewer-doc-light` on the scrolled window and the text view; starts as the window theme, not remembered). Table column widths use the new `unicode-width` dependency, so wide characters (CJK) count two columns; 1 new test. Starts under Xephyr with no GTK warning. NOT checked by eye (colours, switch look).

## 2026-10-13
- Feature (viewer): Markdown view gets inline pictures, real tables and search. Pictures are child widgets in the text (`Run.image`; local files relative to the folder of the file, `%20` decoded, scaled to 800 px; web addresses and unreadable files fall back to `[image: alt]`, the error is logged). A table is drawn as a box (`┌─┬─┐`) in monospace without wrapping, columns padded by character count and aligned left / center / right as the file says, bold header, cell styles and links kept; a picture in a cell stays text. Because the table is text, the search finds it. Search reuses the PDF bar (the Pages button is hidden): Ctrl+F, Enter / F3, Shift+Enter / Shift+F3, Esc, `3/17` label, matches tinted with the `match` / `current-match` tags. Limit: wide characters (CJK, emoji) misalign a table because the width is counted in characters. 4 new parser tests (16 in `markdown.rs`). Starts under Xephyr with a picture, a missing picture and a table, no GTK warning. NOT checked by eye (look of tables and pictures, search scrolling).
- Feature (viewer, ipc): `opusdm-viewer` shows Markdown files (`.md`, `.markdown`) rendered, with the new `pulldown-cmark` dependency. `markdown.rs` turns the text into styled runs (headings, bold / italic / strikethrough, inline and block code, bullet / numbered / task lists, quotes, rules, links, tables as `|` rows, image as `[image: alt]`, raw HTML dropped; 9 unit tests); `markdown_view.rs` shows them in a read-only `TextView` with tags, clickable links (hand pointer, opened with the desktop) and a limit of 8 MiB. New `MediaKind::Markdown`: folders bring `.md` files into the gallery, the strip shows `MD`, zoom / rotate keys are off for it and the arrows scroll. New default file type **Markdown file** (`*.md;*.markdown`), only for a new `filetypes.json`. Tests added in `kind.rs`, `keys.rs` and `filetypes.rs`; the viewer starts on a `.md` file under Xephyr with no GTK warning. NOT checked by eye (look of the text, link clicks).
- Feature (shot): the selection of `opusdm-shot` is no longer taken on mouse release. It stays on screen with a grip at the middle of each side; dragging a side (or two at once on a corner) resizes it, with the matching resize cursor on hover, dragging outside draws a new one, **Enter** keeps it and **Esc** cancels. New in `selection.rs`: `Edges`, `hit_edges`, `resize` (4 unit tests, 52 pass in the crate). NOT checked by eye in the GUI.
- Feature (ipc, hub, lister): new internal command **New Lister** (`InternalCmd::NewLister`) with an optional `path` (template, `~` and relative paths like Change Directory, must be a folder). Without a path the hub opens a Lister on its default folder. Offered in the Function Editor; 2 new tests in `functions/tests.rs`. NOT checked by eye in the GUI.
- Fix (hub): a function or `exec:` command (e.g. `Ctrl+Print` -> `opusdm-shot`) failed with `cannot start 'x': No such file or directory` when the active Lister was inside an archive (`a.zip#dir`), on a remote `sftp://` / `ftp://` folder or in a deleted folder: that path was used as the child's working directory and `spawn` returned ENOENT. `usable_cwd` now walks up to the nearest real folder (else `$HOME`). 4 new tests in `functions/tests.rs`; the regression test fails without the fix. NOT checked by eye with the real hotkey.
- Feature (lister): `Ctrl+L` focuses the path bar with its whole text selected (`Action::FocusPath`, `Ui::focus_path_entry`), from the list or from any text field. Test added in `keys.rs`. NOT checked by eye in the GUI.
- Fix (lister): in the path bar, `Enter` with a look-ahead suggestion showing now goes to the completed folder (`/home/fabio/Do` + Enter opens `/home/fabio/Documents`). Before, the suggestion was deleted and the truncated text was not found. NOT checked by eye in the GUI.
- Feature (lister): the path bar expands a leading `~` (alone or `~/...`) to `$HOME`, both on Enter and in the folder look-ahead (`pathcomplete::expand_tilde`). `~user` is left alone. 1 new unit test, `pathcomplete` tests pass. NOT checked by eye in the GUI.

## 2026-10-12
- Change (openbox): new window theme **OpusDMTheme** (`assets/openbox-share/themes/OpusDMTheme/openbox-3/themerc`), a copy of Onyx with `border.width: 4` (was 1) so the window edges are easier to grab for resizing. `openbox-rc.xml` uses it; `build-release.sh` stages it in `dist/share/themes`; `install.sh` and `live-update.sh --config` copy it to `/usr/share/themes` (so root sees it too); `run-x11.sh` and `snap-live-test.sh` put the repo folder first in `XDG_DATA_DIRS`. Checked: `bash -n`, Openbox starts under Xephyr with no message. NOT checked by eye (frame width), nor an installed session.
- Feature (network, network-config, panel, settings): **Network Connections**, a new `opusdm-network-manager` binary (crate `crates/network-config`) like the Connections page of KDE Plasma: the NetworkManager connections grouped as Ethernet, Wi-Fi, VPN and Other with a search field, and an editor with the tabs General (autoconnect, priority, device or WireGuard interface, VPN started with the connection), Wi-Fi / Ethernet / WireGuard / OpenVPN (OpenVPN rare options in an Advanced dialog) and IPv4 / IPv6 (method, addresses, gateway, DNS, ignore automatic DNS, never default). Save only with changes and no validation error (wrong fields outlined in red with the reason as tooltip), Revert, Save / Discard / Cancel when leaving a changed connection or closing, Connect / Disconnect, + (choose the kind), - (with confirmation), Import of WireGuard `.conf` and OpenVPN `.ovpn` files (the editor opens the result unsaved and lists the ignored directives; inline certificates become 0600 files in `~/.local/share/opusdm/vpn/<uuid>/`, written on save and removed with the connection). In `opusdm-network`: `settings` (helpers on the `a{sa{sv}}` map), `ipconfig`, `profile` (typed model; writing starts from the map that was read so unknown keys survive, the legacy `addresses`, `dns`, `cloned-mac-address` keys are dropped because NetworkManager would prefer them; secrets never go into the map, they are agent owned or, for an OpenVPN password, not saved), `wgkey` (x25519 key generation, checked against RFC 7748), `wgconf` and `ovpn` parsers, `import`, `profiles` (`ProfileBackend` with `FakeProfiles`, implemented by `NmBackend`), `watch::spawn_profiles`. The keyring keeps secrets by profile, setting and key with the `nm-applet` attributes (the Wi-Fi keys already kept are still found); the secret agent answers for `vpn` and `wireguard` too and first looks in a one-shot store that holds a password asked for one connect; the program registers its own agent (`org.opusdm.network.manager`). The panel network tray gets a VPN section with a switch per WireGuard / OpenVPN connection (password window for one that asks every time), a lock over the icon and *VPN: name* in the tooltip while a VPN is up, and **Configure connections...**; OpusDM Settings has a **Network** button in its System tab; `build-release.sh` ships the binary and `live-update.sh` stops it. Tests: `opusdm-network` goes from 28 to 80 (round trips, kept keys, flags, validation, parsers, keys, agent answers, keyring), 11 in `opusdm-network-config` (list groups, edit session, worker on the fake), 3 in the panel. Checked against this machine's NetworkManager 1.52: the list and the existing `pipeline-client` OpenVPN connection read correctly; under Xephyr a new WireGuard connection was created, saved, read back with its private key from the keyring, connected (`wg0` up with its address, so the agent gave the key), disconnected and deleted; an imported `.ovpn` was saved with its CA file (0600 in a 0700 folder), its password asked at connect reached the OpenVPN plugin, delete removed the connection and the folder; in the panel a VPN switch brought a test WireGuard connection up and down with the lock on the icon, and Configure connections opened the program. All test connections were removed. NOT checked: a real VPN server (WireGuard or OpenVPN handshake), the file chooser through the desktop portal (only GTK's own chooser, `GTK_USE_PORTAL=0`, under Xephyr), Wi-Fi and Ethernet edits saved to real connections, Wayland. The panel's agent in a second session reports *Not authorized* when another one with the same identifier is registered (seen with the Xephyr sandbox next to the real session). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-network-manager-design.md`; usage in section 3.37 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Feature (hub): new flag `opusdm-hub --no-startup-apps` skips launching the Startup Apps of Settings (`startup_apps::skipped_by_args`). `scripts/live-update.sh --all` passes it (the Startup Apps are still running, only OpusDM processes were stopped) and `run-x11.sh` always passes it (the sandbox must not open the real session's apps). The plain `live-update.sh` (hub only) and the session scripts are unchanged. 1 new unit test, `sh -n` / `bash -n` pass. NOT checked: a real `live-update.sh --all` run.
- Checked (system): after installing the new session scripts and a logout / login, Brave keeps its logins. Brave stored its `Brave Safe Storage` key in `login` (`login/9`); that key decrypts 78/78 cookies and 6/8 `Login Data` rows; `Local State` now has `prev_desktop: OpusDM`; `~/.local/share/keyrings` holds only `login.keyring`, `default` and `user.keystore`; gnome-keyring owns `org.freedesktop.secrets`, `ksecretd` is stopped. The user reports everything works after the logout / login. NOT checked by me: Chrome and Zed (reported working by the user), and whether a new `Default_N` appears later.
- Fix (system, not in the repo): Brave, Chrome and Zed forgot their logins at every session. Their secrets sat in keyrings other than `login` (`Default_6` for Zed, `Default_keyring` for Chrome, four different `Brave Safe Storage` keys in four keyrings), which stay locked because PAM unlocks only `login`; none of the four Brave keys decrypted the current `Login Data` / `Cookies` (Brave seems to use the Secret portal, `Local State` has `os_crypt.portal`). By choice of the user all keyrings except `login.keyring` were deleted (with `default`, which holds `login`, and `user.keystore`), with gnome-keyring stopped, to start again on `login` only. Backup of the whole directory in `~/keyrings-backup-2026-10-12` (mode 700, secrets in it, delete when no longer needed). Everything those keyrings held is gone from the live set (Zed's GitHub token, Chrome's key, the other apps' items): logins must be done again. NOT checked: after the next login, that apps save to `login` and no new `Default_N` appears.
- Change (session): `opusdm-session` and `opusdm-session-wayland` stop `ksecretd`, `kwalletd5` and `kwalletd6` of the user at start (`pkill -x`), so only gnome-keyring answers on `org.freedesktop.secrets` in an OpusDM session; `pam_kwallet5` starts them at every login, Plasma sessions are not touched (the lines are only in the OpusDM scripts). Checked: `sh -n`, and that the `pkill` pattern matches the process name (it stopped the real `ksecretd` of this session). NOT checked: a fresh login with the installed scripts.
- Fix (session, X11): `opusdm-session` now exports `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=OpusDM` and `XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11` and runs `dbus-update-activation-environment --systemd` with them and `DISPLAY`, like the Wayland session. Before, apps such as Brave started in a session with no desktop name, so they could not tell which keyring to use for their "Safe Storage" key (passwords and cookies). Found while looking at Brave forgetting logins: the keyrings hold four different `Brave Safe Storage` keys (`Default_Keyring`, `Default_Keyring_1`, `Default_12`, `Default_keyring`) and the `default` alias pointed to `Default_Keyring`, which gnome-keyring cannot load. The alias was moved to `login` by hand (`SetAlias`, backup in `~/.local/share/keyrings/default.bak-2026-10-12`). NOT done: choosing and removing the extra Brave keys (which one decrypts the current `Login Data` is not checked). NOT checked: a fresh login with the new script, and that the desktop name changes Brave's choice of password store.
- Feature (panel): middle-click on a window in the window list closes it, like its close button (Ctrl+F4); the right-click menu has a new "Close Window" entry before "Force kill". X11 sends `_NET_CLOSE_WINDOW` to the window manager, Wayfire uses `window-rules/close-view` (new `opusdm_wayland::wayfire::close`), other Wayland compositors the foreign-toplevel close request (`Session::close`). Existing tests pass; NOT checked by eye.

## 2026-10-11
- Refactor (panel): the audio, devices, network and battery tray centers share `TransientMessage` (`transient_message.rs`): the error line, its generation token and the 5 s timer that clears it unless a newer message replaced it. Each center keeps a one-line `show_message`. 1 new unit test.
- Test (iconview): the tests that change `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` / `XDG_CACHE_HOME` / `OPUSDM_NO_THUMBS` share one crate-wide lock (`test_env_lock`); before, `icon_set` and `field` had separate locks and the two thumbnail tests none, so `thumbs::cache::store_then_load...` could fail when `thumbs::pool`'s test removed `XDG_CACHE_HOME` mid-run (seen once). 6 runs in a row pass.
- Test (iconview): the 419-line `gtk_widget_behavior` test is split into 17 scenario functions (`icon_item_basics`, `field_selection`, `field_pinned_positions`...). They still run inside one test on one thread (GTK only works on the thread that initialized it), but each runs under `catch_unwind`, so a failure no longer hides the scenarios after it and the assertion names every failing scenario. The scratch `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is now set for the whole run. Checked with a deliberately broken assertion: only that scenario was reported.
- Change (ui, viewer, shot): `chrome_css` now starts with `@define-color opusdm_accent` (name in `opusdm_ui::ACCENT_COLOR_NAME`); the viewer's current-thumbnail outline and the screenshot editor's checked toggles and colour swatch use it instead of the literals `#3584e4` / `#4a90ff` (the screenshot editor blue changes to the OpusDM accent). 1 new unit test; the Lister theme test now checks only its own `theme_bg_color` definition.
- Change (Lister): the File Types window opened on its own reports its save result through `err_log!` (`[opusdm-lister:pid] File Types: ...`) instead of a bare `eprintln!`. Build only, no test (log format).
- Refactor (hub): `Hub::send_to(cid, msg)` and `send_to_lister(cid, msg)` replace 15 copies of "look up the connection, send, ignore the error"; a message to a closing connection is now logged at debug level. One send stays on the field (the job progress path still borrows `self.jobs`). Covered by the existing server tests (Pong, JumpTo, Present, function prompts, QueryConfig).
- Refactor (hub): the job-progress and function-event forwarder threads of `owner_loop` are one `spawn_forwarder(name, rx, owner_tx, wrap)`. 1 new unit test.
- Refactor (ipc, Lister, panel, devices): new `opusdm_ipc::size` with the one scaling loop (`scale`) and the three unit styles in use: `binary_size` (Lister free-space gauge), `decimal_size` (device names, `opusdm_devices::human_size`) and `rate` (panel network meter); the Lister size column reuses `scale`. Same texts as before. `opusdm-devices` now depends on `opusdm-ipc`. 4 new unit tests.
- Refactor (theme-config, Lister, hub): the five longest UI functions are split into builders and handlers, no behaviour change. Settings `build_window` 366 -> 94 lines (`Sections::read`, page builders, `connect_apply` / `connect_import` / `connect_export`); Lister `show_context_menu` 249 lines -> a `Menu` helper (`item`, `separator`) plus `transfer`, `set_background`, `leave_out`; Lister button row `attach` 162 lines -> `on_click` plus small handlers; Theme Editor `open` 165 lines -> `build_layout`, `wire_list_selection`, `wire_close_guard`; hub `apply_event` 144 lines -> `reassert_desktop_windows`, `update_leftout`, `apply_desktop_settings`, `follow_desktop_dir`. A few dropped send errors (Leave Out, Make Directory, New File, role lock) now flash a message. Existing tests pass; GUI NOT checked by eye.
- Refactor (ui): `LayerSpec::new(layer, namespace)` with neutral defaults, chained setters (`anchors`, `margins`, `exclusive`, `keyboard`, `monitor`) and the presets `background(namespace, rect)` and `modal_overlay(namespace)`. The desktop, panel, device popup, dock, Run window and polkit dialog build their layer spec with them instead of spelling out every field. Same specs as before. 2 new unit tests.
- Fix (Lister, hub): the stand-alone Manage Functions, Edit Hotkeys and File Types windows start from the hub's copy of the Functions and bindings instead of reading `functions.json` / `hotkeys.json` from disk, which could be stale while the hub's persist thread was still writing. New `ToHub::QueryConfig`: the hub answers the asking connection (registered or not) with `BindingsUpdate` + `FunctionsUpdate`; `client::fetch_config` / `ConfigReply` collect the answer. Edit Hotkeys Save now keeps the hub's user menu and script events instead of re-reading them from disk. File Types falls back to disk when no hub answers. 3 new unit tests (hub answer, reply collection, fetch over a socket). NOT checked by eye.
- Perf (viewer): the PDF page worker and the page-panel thumbnail worker keep one hayro `RenderCache` per open document (`render_page_cached`), so fonts and outlines are not decoded again at every page turn. The page worker is now a loop per document (`_serve_document`) because the cache borrows the document. 1 new unit test (cached and fresh renders give the same pixels), the worker test also covers switching documents.
- Fix (Lister): the archive preview of a damaged zip or tar showed an empty listing. `listing_text` now checks the exit status of `unzip -l` / `tar -tf` and shows the tool's error (or the status) instead. 2 new unit tests.
- Fix (Lister, fsops): browsing, previewing and extracting from 7z archives in the Lister now work on systems with only `7zz` or `7za`; the Lister ran a literal `7z`. New `opusdm_fsops::tools` (`SEVEN_Z_TOOLS`, `find_in_path`, `seven_z_tool`, `seven_z_program`) replaces the two private resolvers of `compress.rs` and `extract.rs`, and the Lister uses it. 3 new unit tests.
- Perf (Lister): browsing the folders of an archive no longer runs `zipinfo` / `tar -t` / `7z l` over the whole file at every step. `archive::ListingCache` keeps the parsed listings of the last 4 archives, valid while the size and mtime of the file are unchanged; `read_archive_rows` filters the cached listing by folder. 2 new unit tests.
- Change (Lister): `opusdm-viewer` is now the only image viewer. **Show** (F-17) opens the selected pictures in it, and **Read (SmartRead)** (also from the Find dialog) sends the pictures of the selection to it and opens only the other files in the Lister Viewer. The Lister Viewer loses its picture mode, the mark-for-delete keys (`Delete` / `m`), the delete-on-close question and `slideshow.rs`; SmartRead on a picture asked for by name shows it as hex. New `image_viewer.rs` (`split_images`, `viewer_command` that prefers the binary next to the Lister, `launch`). 2 new unit tests, Viewer key tests updated, the image load test removed. Guide and FEATURES.md updated. NOT checked by eye.
- Refactor (hub): the five Function-run maps of `Hub` (pending runs, selection waiters, requesters, cancel tokens, next run id) plus the executor event sender move into `FunctionRuns` (`server/function_runs.rs`), which owns the selection matching and the clean-up of a dropped connection. `Hub` goes from 42 to 37 fields. 5 new unit tests that need no hub. The jobs / prompts grouping is not done yet.
- Refactor (ui): `opusdm_ui::x11_window_id(&impl IsA<gtk::Native>)` replaces the seven private copies of the surface to X11 xid downcast (hub, panel window and device popup, dock, runner, polkit, buttonbank); `gdk4-x11` is now a dependency of `opusdm-ui` only. 1 new unit test.
- Refactor (ui): new `opusdm_ui::app_css` / `install_app_css(app_name, extra_css)`; the nine stand-alone programs (player, runner, shot, viewer, apps-editor, input-config, screen, terminal, polkit) call it instead of each building its own CssProvider from `chrome_css` + `ui_look_css`. 1 new unit test.
- Fix (Lister): opening a file inside an archive no longer freezes the Lister while `unzip` / `tar` / `7z` runs. New `archive::extract_single_file_in_background` runs `extract_single_file_temp` on a worker thread and hands the result over an `async_channel`; `activate_row` awaits it and now flashes extraction and launch errors (before they were dropped silently). 1 new unit test (OPTIMIZE.md item).
- Docs: new `OPTIMIZE.md`, a prioritised optimization / refactoring checklist (1 critical, 4 high, 7 medium, 6 low items) from a codebase review. No code changed.
- Fix (viewer): zooming out below the window size stretched the picture (seen on PDF pages, e.g. at 21% the page filled the window width). In the fixed scale mode `gtk::Picture` is centred with `ContentFit::Fill`, and with `Align::Center` it gets its natural size, which was the size of the texture: larger than the zoomed picture, so it took the whole window. `nearest.rs` becomes `view_paintable.rs`: `ViewPaintable` reports the size on screen as its intrinsic size (`zoom::screen_size`) and draws with the trilinear filter (smooth, better for small text when zoomed out) or nearest neighbour (sharp pixels); `ImageView` gives the picture a new one at every zoom change. 1 new unit test. Checked in Xephyr: an 11 page PDF zoomed out to 21% keeps its proportions, also rotated, and zooming in to 107% scrolls as before.
- Change (viewer): on a PDF the arrow keys turn pages: **Left** / **Up** previous page, **Right** / **Down** next page (before, Left / Right moved to the previous / next file and Up / Down did nothing). **Space**, **n** and **p** still move through the files. `keys::map` maps them to `PrevPage` / `NextPage` for `MediaKind::Pdf` only; pictures and videos keep their arrows. 1 new unit test.
- Change (viewer): `opusdm-viewer` shows what its command line names instead of the whole folder of one file. `opusdm-viewer <file or folder>...`: a file is shown alone (the thumbnail strip is hidden while the gallery has one file, also after a delete leaves one, and the info bar drops the `1/1` position); several files show only those files, in the order given; a folder brings its pictures, PDF files and videos (no hidden files, no sub folders) in natural order; files and folders mix, a file named twice is kept once, the first one opens. `Gallery::build` is replaced by `Gallery::from_targets` (`_folder_items` for a folder; an empty result is an error); `args::parse` returns every argument and `check_target` accepts folders. A Lister double click (`exec:opusdm-viewer {f}`) now shows only the clicked file. 5 new gallery tests replace the 3 of `build`, args tests rewritten. Checked in Xephyr: one picture with no strip and no other file of its folder; a picture plus a folder shows the picture and the two of the folder; a missing name exits with status 1.
- Feature (viewer): page panel, text search and links for PDF documents in `opusdm-viewer`. A bar over a PDF (`pdf_bar.rs`) has a **Pages** toggle (also **F9**) and a search field (**Ctrl+F**) with previous / next buttons and the match count. Page panel (`pdf_pages.rs`): one button per page on the left, thumbnails rendered by a worker thread only once the panel opens, from the current page on, stopped when the document changes. Search: `pdf_text.rs` interprets a page with a hayro `Device` that only keeps the glyphs with their Unicode and boxes (page points, y down) and lays them out with spaces and new lines; `PageText::find` is case-insensitive, folds white space and gives one box per line; `pdf_search.rs` runs it in a worker that keeps the text of the pages already read and drops a search when a newer one arrives, and `Hits` moves through the matches (wrapping). Matches are tinted on a copy of the page (`RawImage::highlighted`, yellow, current orange); Enter / F3 next, Shift+Enter / Shift+F3 previous, Esc clears; going to a match turns the page and, when zoomed, scrolls to it (`ImageView::reveal`). Links (`pdf_links.rs`): `/Link` annotations with a URI action, a GoTo action or a `/Dest`, explicit (`[page /XYZ ...]`) or named (catalog `/Dests` or the `/Names /Dests` tree); a click goes to the page or opens the URI with `gtk::UriLauncher`, the pointer becomes a hand and the tooltip tells the target. View to picture mapping in `zoom.rs` (`content_box`, `rotate_point`, `unrotate_point`) and `ImageView::image_point`. The PDF part of the window moved to `window/pdf.rs`. 18 new unit tests (layout, search, ligatures, text of a real page, links of every kind, link lookup, matches, search worker, thumbnail worker and order, highlight, geometry). Checked in Xephyr: the panel opens with F9 and shows the thumbnails, Ctrl+F "engineer" on an 11 page paper gives 72 matches tinted on the page, the tooltip "Go to page 13" over a reference of an arXiv paper and the click goes to page 13. NOT checked: opening a web link (it would start the real browser), search keys Shift+Enter / F3 / Esc and the bar in full screen.
- Feature (viewer): `opusdm-viewer` shows PDF documents. Pages are rendered with `hayro` 0.8 (pure Rust, Apache-2.0/MIT, no system library; it needs Rust 1.92, so the workspace `rust-version` goes from 1.91 to 1.92). New `MediaKind::Pdf` (extension `pdf`), so PDF files join the gallery of the folder. New `crates/viewer/src/pdf.rs`: `open`, `render_page` (white background, 2 px per point = 144 dpi, longest edge capped at 8192 px), `render_thumb` (first page, for the strip) and a page worker thread that keeps the last opened document and renders only the newest request. A page is a `RawImage`, so zoom, drag, rotation and the sharp-pixel switch work as for pictures. Keys (PDF only): **Page Down** / **Page Up** next / previous page, **Home** / **End** first / last page (`keys::page_after`); zoom and rotation stay across pages (`ImageView::replace_image`) and the view goes back to the top. Info bar shows `page 2/10`. Slideshow shows a PDF for 4 seconds like a picture. New default file type **PDF file** (`*.pdf`, double click `exec:opusdm-viewer {f}`); existing `filetypes.json` files are not changed. Limits: no text selection or search, no links, password protected files unsupported, zooming past the 144 dpi render gets soft. 10 new unit tests (rendering, missing page, damaged file, thumbnail size, scale cap, worker, page keys, page moves, default PDF type), plus PDF cases in the kind, slideshow and file type tests. Checked: 25 real PDFs from `~/Downloads` (1 to 791 pages, up to 6.5 MB) all rendered, first page in 2 to 95 ms in release except one image-heavy file (677 ms); the only all-white page is blank in `pdftoppm` too; the debug binary starts on a real PDF without errors. NOT checked by eye: the window with a PDF (page keys, info bar, thumbnails).
- Change (Lister): the button row starts with a **Parent Directory** button (up arrow, same as the navigation bar one), before Copy; it calls `commands::go_parent`, so archives and remote folders work as with the nav bar button.
- Change (Theme Editor): the editor opens on the active theme (`settings.json` `theme.icon_set`; the first theme when it is not installed), also after Delete. The default theme, which cannot be changed, no longer shows its tabs: the right side shows only a message pointing to Duplicate / Save as copy, and Save, Revert, Save & Apply and Delete are disabled. The theme open in the editor is previewed live on the running desktop: a 250 ms timer resolves the widgets' draft (`apply::resolve_draft`, pictures taken as the draft's absolute paths) and sends it to the hub with `SetSettings` when it changed (`model::send_preview`, nothing is written when no hub runs). Closing the editor sends back the settings in force when it opened; Save of the active theme and Save & Apply move that baseline to the saved look, Save of another theme does not. `apply_theme` takes the baseline as preferences instead of re-reading `settings.json`, which now holds the preview. Note: the hub persists every `SetSettings`, so if the editor crashes the preview stays in `settings.json`. 1 unit test. Checked in Xephyr with a fake hub on the socket: the active theme "My Theme" selected at open; the default theme shows the message; toggling Type indicators sent a preview, Close + discard sent the original settings back; selecting Breeze Dark previewed it and Close restored "My Theme". NOT checked: Save and Save & Apply against a real hub, the Lister repainting.
- Feature (polkit): new `opusdm-polkit`, a polkit authentication agent (crate `crates/polkit`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-polkit-agent-design.md`). Before, `pkexec` programs such as Synaptic started from `opusdm-run` or the menu failed silently: no agent was registered for the session, so polkitd refused without asking for a password. The agent registers on the system bus for the login session (`XDG_SESSION_ID`, logind fallback), serves `org.freedesktop.PolicyKit1.AuthenticationAgent` (only polkitd may call it) and shows one GTK dialog per request: icon, message, user choice (group identities expanded to their members, current user first), the PAM prompt, errors, details; a wrong password starts a new run of `polkit-agent-helper-1` and keeps the dialog open; `CancelAuthentication` closes it. Wayland: centered overlay layer surface with exclusive keyboard; X11: centered on the primary monitor, kept above. The hub starts it at login (a second instance exits through GApplication uniqueness); `build-release.sh` ships it and `live-update.sh --all` restarts it. 13 unit tests (helper protocol and `g_strcompress` unescaping, a fake helper for success, failure and a helper that dies, identity expansion and ordering, centering). Checked: the debug binary registers with the real polkitd 126 and stays up. NOT checked: a real password dialog end to end (`pkexec`, Synaptic), Wayland.
- Feature (panel): the battery tray reads the screen brightness again every time its popover opens, so the slider follows changes made by keys or other programs. `opusdm-brightness`: the worker takes a read command besides set (`Controller::read`) and answers with the new `Event::Current`; a burst of commands applies the last set, then reads once. Checked: unit tests; in Xephyr, brightness changed to 50% through logind with the panel running, the reopened popover showed 50% (then set back to 30%).
- Feature (panel): battery tray next to the device, audio and network trays, in the spirit of the KDE Plasma battery applet. The icon is a battery filled to its level (red at 15% or less when unplugged) with a bolt over it on mains power; the tooltip gives level, state and time left. The popover shows level and state (Charging, Discharging, Fully charged, "Plugged in, not charging"), a level bar, time until empty or full, battery health (UPower `Capacity`), the power profile (Power Save / Balanced / Performance through power-profiles-daemon, hidden without it) and a display brightness slider (the `opusdm-brightness` worker, started only once a battery is found; hidden when no method works). The tray is hidden on machines without a battery. New crate `opusdm-power` (UPower and power-profiles-daemon over the system bus, no GTK, with a `power_state` example); `opusdm-watch` gains `signal_ticks_any` to wake on signals from several bus names. Checked: unit tests; `power_state` example against the real UPower; in Xephyr the icon shows, the popover lists 93% / Fully charged / health 73% / profiles / brightness, and a click on Power Save switched the real profile (then set back to Balanced). NOT checked live: the discharging and charging states (laptop stayed plugged in and full), and that the brightness slider follows changes made elsewhere (it does not: it shows the value read at start and the user's own moves).
- Fix (scripts): `dist/install.sh` run while OpusDM is running from the prefix stopped at `cp: cannot create regular file '/opt/opusdm/bin/opusdm-hub': Text file busy` (`set -e`), before registering any session, so no OpusDM entry appeared in SDDM. Binaries and session scripts are now copied as `<name>.new` and renamed over the old file (`install` + `mv -f`, as `live-update.sh` does); running processes keep the old file. Checked: `cp` over a running test binary fails with "Text file busy" while `install` + `mv` succeeds; dry run with a fake `sudo` writes `opusdm-wayfire.desktop` and `opusdm-labwc.desktop`. NOT checked: the real install.
- Change (scripts, Wayland): `dist/install.sh --wayland` now takes the compositor, `--wayland labwc` or `--wayland wayfire` (given twice, both are registered). Before copying anything it stops when the chosen compositor is not installed (`command -v`), instead of only warning. Each compositor gets its own login entry, `/usr/share/wayland-sessions/opusdm-<compositor>.desktop`, named **OpusDM (labwc)** or **OpusDM (wayfire)**; the old single `opusdm-wayland.desktop` entry is removed. The entry template has a `@COMPOSITOR@` placeholder and its `Exec` passes the compositor to `opusdm-session-wayland`, which now takes `labwc|wayfire` as its argument (rejects any other, exit 2); without it the old choice stays (`OPUSDM_COMPOSITOR`, else Wayfire when installed, else labwc). Checked: `sh -n` on the generated scripts; `install.sh` with no session, `--wayland` with no value, `--wayland sway`, and a compositor missing from `PATH` all stop before installing; the entry generated for labwc; the session script with a fake `labwc`/`wayfire` runs the right one for each argument and the old choice without one. NOT checked: a real install and login from SDDM.

## 2026-10-10
- Fix (scripts, Wayfire): after `live-update.sh` the Input window still said OpusDM cannot set the input settings under Wayfire. The script copies only a list of variables from the running hub to the new one, and `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` and `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE` were not in it, so the restarted hub and everything it starts (Input settings, panel pager) did not know it ran under Wayfire. Both are now copied; a hub that already lost them gets them back from Wayfire's IPC socket `/tmp/wayfire-$WAYLAND_DISPLAY.socket` and the per-user `~/.config/opusdm/wayfire.ini`. Checked: `sh -n`, and the variable block run against the running hub's environment gives `/tmp/wayfire-wayland-2.socket` and `~/.config/opusdm/wayfire.ini`. NOT checked: a full live update.
- Feature (Wayland, input settings, Wayfire): `opusdm-input-config` and the hub's input thread now work under Wayfire too (before, the window only said the settings belong to the compositor). New `opusdm_wayland::wayfire_ini` (`config_path` from `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE`, which Wayfire sets for its children, else `~/.config/wayfire.ini`; `with_block` puts a `#` marked block at the end of an INI section, adding the section when missing; 3 unit tests); `labwc::env_markers` and `replace_marked` are now shared with it. New `crates/input-config/src/wayfire.rs` (`WayfireBackend`, 2 unit tests): mouse, touchpad, layouts, variants, XKB options and key repeat become one block of `[input]` lines (`mouse_cursor_speed`, `mouse_accel_profile`, `left_handed_mode`, `mouse_natural_scroll`, `touchpad_cursor_speed`, `tap_to_click`, `natural_scroll`, `disable_touchpad_while_typing`, `xkb_layout`, `xkb_variant`, `xkb_options`, `kb_repeat_rate`, `kb_repeat_delay`). `for_session` picks it when `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` is there; `session_problem` accepts labwc and Wayfire. Wayfire's `left_handed_mode` covers every pointer, so Left handed also swaps the touchpad. Not done through the IPC `wayfire/set-config-options`: it locks the options, so later hand edits of the file would be ignored. Checked in a headless Wayfire 0.9: `WAYFIRE_CONFIG_FILE` names the `-c` file, an atomic rewrite is reloaded, the last of two equal keys wins; a test program through `for_session` wrote the block after the user's `xkb_layout = de` and `wayfire/get-config-option` then read `it,us`, `,intl`, `grp:alt_shift_toggle`, repeat 55 / 321, mouse speed 0.4. NOT checked: the window itself and a real device in a Wayfire login.
- Fix (Wayland session, Wayfire): at login the screen stayed grey and every GTK window (Settings, Lister) appeared only about 25 s later, which looked like a missing refresh fixed by a VT switch. GTK4 reads the Settings portal (light/dark) before showing its first window, and the call timed out: Wayfire, unlike labwc, does not hand `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` to D-Bus and systemd, so `xdg-desktop-portal-gtk` could not open a display, and with no wlr backend the portal also waited for the KDE one, which does not answer outside Plasma. Wayfire now starts `opusdm-session-wayland --hub`, which runs `dbus-update-activation-environment --systemd` (WAYLAND_DISPLAY, DISPLAY, XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP, XDG_SESSION_TYPE, WAYFIRE_SOCKET), restarts the portals left from an earlier session (`systemctl --user try-restart`) and then execs the hub. New `opusdm-portals.conf` (GTK backend for the OpusDM desktop) shipped in `config/` and copied once to `~/.config/xdg-desktop-portal/` by the session script, for labwc too. Checked in the running Wayfire session by hand (same commands): `Settings.ReadOne` went from a 25 s timeout to 0.1 s, a new Lister was mapped 0.26 s after the request. The session script checked with fake commands (session and `--hub` mode). NOT checked: a fresh login with the installed script.
- Fix (Wayfire): `wayfire.ini` gave the title bar colours as `#2b2d30ff`, but in Wayfire's config `#` starts a comment, so both values were empty ("invalid option value" in the log) and the defaults were used. They are now escaped (`\#2b2d30ff`). Checked: Wayfire reloaded the file with no new error. The per-user copy `~/.config/opusdm/wayfire.ini` needs the same edit.
- Feature (Wayland, Wayfire): OpusDM supports Wayfire next to labwc. New `opusdm_wayland::wayfire` speaks Wayfire's IPC (plugins `ipc` and `ipc-rules`, socket from `WAYFIRE_SOCKET`, length-prefixed JSON): `State` with the views and outputs (`desktop_count`, `current_desktop`, `active`, `desktop_of`, `rect_of`, workspaces numbered row by row), `watch` for live changes, `focus`, `set_minimized`, `switch_desktop`, `move_to_desktop`; `examples/wayfire.rs` prints the state and moves a window. Under Wayfire the panel's `wl_windows` uses it instead of the Wayland protocols, so the pager draws the real window boxes on the right desktop and **Move to desktop** works; it falls back to the protocols when the IPC cannot be watched. New `wayfire.ini` (4 desktops in a row, no wayfire-shell, the hub started through `$OPUSDM_HUB`, vswitch bindings, close button first); `run-wayland.sh --wayfire` runs a nested Wayfire with it; `build-release.sh` ships it as `config/wayfire.ini`, and the Wayland session script picks Wayfire when installed (else labwc, `OPUSDM_COMPOSITOR` forces one) and seeds `~/.config/opusdm/wayfire.ini` once. 11 unit tests. Checked in a real Wayfire 0.9 login: desktop, panel and dock are mapped layer surfaces and show. The first login showed a grey screen and windows that appeared late: see the portal fix above.
- Fix (Wayfire): GTK4 windows (Settings, Lister...) had no title bar and no buttons. GTK4 asks for server side decorations through `org_kde_kwin_server_decoration` and Wayfire 0.9 grants them, but its decoration plugin then draws nothing for those windows (it does frame xdg-decoration clients such as Konsole). `wayfire.ini` now sets `preferred_decoration_mode = client`, so GTK draws its own title bar. Checked in the running Wayfire session: a new Lister shows the GTK title bar with minimize, maximize and close. The per-user copy `~/.config/opusdm/wayfire.ini` is not refreshed by the session script, update it by hand.
- Change (scripts, Wayfire): the Wayland session script sends Wayfire's output to `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/opusdm/wayfire.log` (previous run kept as `wayfire.log.1`) instead of the login manager's log, and starts Wayfire with `-d` when `OPUSDM_WAYFIRE_DEBUG=1` or the file `~/.config/opusdm/wayfire-debug` exists. For the open problem of Wayfire showing new frames only after a VT switch. Checked with a fake `wayfire`: plain run, debug by file, debug by variable, log rotation. NOT checked in a real login.
- Fix (panel, Wayland): **Logout** did nothing on Wayland, it always ran `openbox --exit`. It now asks the compositor to quit (`SIGTERM`, what `labwc --exit` does; Wayfire handles it too), which ends the session since the session script `exec`s the compositor. New `opusdm_wayland::compositor::exit`: the pid comes from `LABWC_PID`, else from the peer credentials (`SO_PEERCRED`) of the Wayland socket, so it works under Wayfire and any other compositor. X11 still runs `openbox --exit`. 1 unit test (peer pid). NOT checked in a running labwc or Wayfire session.
- Change (panel, Wayland): the pager draws a cascaded placeholder box for every window on its desktop, since the compositor does not tell where a window is (`placeholder_rect` in `widgets/pager.rs`). X11 keeps the real rectangles. Unit test added; not checked by eye in labwc.
- Change (labwc): `labwc-rc.xml` puts the window buttons on the left of the titlebar (`<titlebar><layout>close,iconify,max:</layout>`), close first. Not checked in a running labwc.
- Change (scripts, Wayland): `build-release.sh` also ships `config/labwc-rc.xml`, a Wayland session script `opusdm-session-wayland` (exports `PATH`, seeds `~/.config/labwc/rc.xml` from the OpusDM one only when the user has none, then `exec labwc -s opusdm-hub`; `-s`, not `-S`, so restarting the hub does not end the session) and two login entries, **OpusDM (X11)** (`opusdm.desktop`) and **OpusDM (Wayland)** (`opusdm-wayland.desktop`). `dist/install.sh` now requires `--x11` and/or `--wayland` (prefix still optional): the X11 entry goes to `/usr/share/xsessions`, the Wayland one to `/usr/share/wayland-sessions`, both read by SDDM; it warns when openbox, labwc or wlr-randr are missing. `live-update.sh` takes the whole session environment of the running hub (`DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `LABWC_PID`, `GDK_BACKEND`, D-Bus address...; a variable the hub lacks is unset), tells X11 from Wayland the way OpusDM does, restarts the hub in that session, and with `--config` also installs `opusdm-session-wayland` and reloads labwc (`SIGHUP`) instead of Openbox on Wayland. Checked: `sh -n` on both scripts and on the generated `install.sh` and session script; `install.sh` without a session flag or with an unknown one stops with the usage; the environment import on a fake Wayland hub (stale `DISPLAY` dropped) and on one forced to X11. NOT checked: a real install and login from SDDM, a real live update.
- Docs (Wayland): `docs/WAYLAND-PORT.md` marked implemented, with a "What works where" table (X11, labwc/sway, KDE, GNOME) and the known gaps; `docs/usage.md` describes the input and screen settings on Wayland; `OpusDM-Features-Guide.md` mentions `run-wayland.sh` and the packages it needs. Checked: the same build runs on X11 (Xephyr with openbox inside a headless labwc, hub forced to X11): desktop `_NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE_DESKTOP`, panel `DOCK`, dock `NORMAL`, 4 EWMH desktops, snap watcher on, and the screen looks as before.
- Feature (Wayland, input settings): under labwc `opusdm-input-config` and the hub's input thread write the settings into labwc's configuration, since a Wayland client cannot set input devices. New shared `opusdm_wayland::labwc` (`under_labwc`, `rc_path`/`environment_path` with `OPUSDM_LABWC_RC`, `with_block` for marked blocks in `rc.xml` at the end of `<keyboard>` or of `<labwc_config>`, `with_env_block` for marked blocks in `environment`, `write_if_changed`, `reload` by `SIGHUP`; 5 unit tests); the global hotkeys of the hub now use it too (the marker text changed to "written by OpusDM"). New `crates/input-config/src/labwc.rs` (`LabwcBackend`): mouse and touchpad as a `<libinput>` block (categories `non-touch` and `touchpad`, so later devices get them too), key repeat as a block inside `<keyboard>`, layouts, variants and XKB options as `XKB_DEFAULT_*` lines of `environment`; it starts from the saved `input.json` and labwc reloads after every change that alters a file. `backend::SessionBackend` and `for_session` pick it on Wayland; `session_problem` refuses only a session with no display, or Wayland without labwc. 3 unit tests. Checked in a headless labwc: the Input window opens with its three tabs. NOT checked: changing a value from the window and labwc applying it to a real device.
- Feature (Wayland, screens): `opusdm-screen` and the hub's hot plug work on wlroots compositors through `wlr-randr` (package `wlr-randr`). New `crates/screen/src/wlr.rs`: `parse_json` reads `wlr-randr --json` into the same model (one mode per size with its rates, the id from make/model/serial or the output name, the transform as rotation, the Wayland scale turned into its inverse since it works the other way round from `xrandr --scale`, no primary), `wlr_randr_args` sets the whole layout in one call (`--output N --off`, or `--on --mode WxH[@RHz] --pos X,Y --transform T --scale S`). New `WlrRandrBackend` and `backend::for_session`; `session_problem` now only complains with no display at all. On Wayland the hub's GDK monitor watcher calls `screen_hotplug::monitors_changed`, and the hot plug thread reapplies the saved profile after the same 0.5 s settle time. "Primary monitor" is hidden on Wayland. 3 unit tests (JSON, call, transforms). Checked in a headless labwc: the `query` example reads the output, the Screens window shows it, and a `wlr-randr` call in the generated form rotated and scaled it (read back as `Left`, scale 0.5). NOT checked: Apply from the window, real monitors, hot plug.
- Feature (Wayland, shot): `opusdm-shot` captures through the compositor's screen copy on Wayland (`capture::compose` over `opusdm_wayland::screencopy::capture_outputs`): an output placed exactly at the monitor is taken whole at its own resolution (HiDPI kept), otherwise the outputs are pasted at their places. The overlay already used `fullscreen_on_monitor`, which works on Wayland. 2 unit tests. Checked in a headless labwc: the overlay opens full screen with the dimmed capture of the desktop. NOT checked: selecting an area and the editor.
- Feature (Wayland, step 4 of `docs/WAYLAND-PORT.md`): global hotkeys on Wayland (new `crates/hub/src/global_keys_wl.rs`). Under **labwc** (`LABWC_PID` set) the global hotkeys become labwc keybinds (`labwc_key`: `Ctrl+Alt+u` -> `C-A-u`) running `opusdm-hub --run FUNCTION --sock SOCK`, kept in a marked block (`BLOCK_BEGIN`/`BLOCK_END`) at the end of `<keyboard>` of `~/.config/labwc/rc.xml` (`OPUSDM_LABWC_RC` overrides the path; `run-wayland.sh` points it at the sandbox config): `with_block` replaces or removes the block in place, creates `<keyboard>` with `<default />` (or the whole file) when missing, never touches the rest; the file is written only when it changes, atomically, and labwc is sent `SIGHUP` (what `labwc --reconfigure` does). Elsewhere the **GlobalShortcuts portal** (`org.freedesktop.portal.GlobalShortcuts`, zbus): `CreateSession` and `BindShortcuts` (triggers in the XDG format, `portal_trigger`: `CTRL+ALT+u`) through `Request`/`Response`, a new session at every change, `Activated` presses mapped back to their function. New `ToHub::RunFunctionHeadless` (runs a function as an X11 global hotkey does) and `opusdm-hub --run FUNCTION [--sock PATH]` (new `remote.rs`) that sends it and exits. `docs/usage.md` updated. 11 unit tests (key names, block text and quoting, block placement, CLI). Checked in a headless labwc: the hub wrote the block with the 5 default global hotkeys into the sandbox `rc.xml`, the `Ctrl+\` command taken from it opened an `opusdm-terminal` window, `--run builtin-desktop-next` switched to workspace 2. NOT checked: a real key press (no virtual keyboard in the headless session), the portal (it would open a confirmation dialog on the real KDE desktop).
- Feature (Wayland, hub): the hub uses the compositor's window list (`opusdm_wayland`) where it read EWMH. **Start in Tray** (new `tray_start_wl.rs`): Wayland does not say which process owns a window, so the first new window whose app id fits the program name (`app_id_matches`: equal, or a dot separated part equal to the name or to its part before `-`/`_`, generic parts like `org`/`desktop` ignored; `telegram-desktop` fits `org.telegram.desktop`) is minimized and closed up to 3 times until it leaves the list, with the X11 timings; `startup_apps.rs` picks the X11 or Wayland watch (`TrayWatch`). **Full screen detection** (background rotation and videos pause): visible full screen windows cover the GDK monitors whose connector names their outputs (`fullscreen_rects`); the list does not tell workspaces, so a full screen window on another workspace counts too. **Previous/next virtual desktop** functions switch the compositor's workspace. The number of virtual screens is set in the compositor's own config on Wayland (labwc: `<desktops number>`). 3 unit tests. NOT checked by eye.
- Feature (wayland): screenshots on Wayland without external tools. New `opusdm_wayland::screencopy::capture_outputs` copies every output through `zwlr_screencopy_manager_v1` into a `memfd` shared memory buffer and returns opaque RGBA with the output's name and position (`OutputImage`); pure `to_rgba` (XRGB/ARGB/XBGR/ABGR 8888, stride padding, `y_invert`) with 3 unit tests; `examples/screenshot.rs` writes one PPM per output. Checked in a headless labwc with the hub running: the first screenshot of OpusDM on Wayland shows the desktop (background and icons), the top panel with the 4-workspace pager and the SNI tray, and the dock centered on the right edge.
- Feature (Wayland, step 3 of `docs/WAYLAND-PORT.md`): new crate `opusdm-wayland` (no GTK, the counterpart of `opusdm-x11`). `shared()` opens, on first use and only in a Wayland session, a connection of its own served by a thread that follows the windows of every client (`zwlr_foreign_toplevel_manager_v1`: title, app id, maximized, minimized, active, full screen, outputs) and the workspaces (`ext_workspace_manager_v1`), and publishes a `Snapshot` to its subscribers after every `done`; `activate`, `minimize`, `close`, `activate_workspace`, `switch_workspace` go out on the same connection. Pure model (`apply_states`, `pager_order`, `wrapped_index`) with 4 unit tests, and an `examples/watch.rs` tool. The panel's `ewmh::spawn` and its click actions use it on Wayland through the new `wl_windows.rs` (`events` turns snapshots into the same `EwmhEvent`s, only what changed; 2 unit tests), so the tasklist and the pager are unchanged. Wayland does not tell icons, pids, window positions or the workspace of a window: the tasklist takes the icon of the desktop file named by the app id (`ClientWin::app_id`), Force kill and Move to desktop are not offered, every window counts as on every workspace. Checked in a headless labwc 0.8.3 with the `watch` example: 4 workspaces, the switch to the next one, and an `opusdm-terminal` window (title, app id `org.opusdm.Terminal`, active, output `HEADLESS-1`). The panel itself NOT checked by eye.
- Feature (Wayland, button banks): a Wayland client can neither place a normal window nor read its position, so on a compositor with the layer shell a **borderless** bank is a layer surface (new `crates/buttonbank/src/layer.rs`): BOTTOM layer like the dock, anchored to the top left of its monitor, its saved position turned into margins, so the bank always knows where it is and saves it on close and at hub shutdown. With no title bar it moves with **Alt+drag** (the drag is claimed, the buttons do not see it); the new position applies on release and stays on a monitor. A bank without a saved position is centered on the first monitor. Banks **with a title bar** stay normal windows placed by the compositor; their saved position is kept in the file but not applied (Wayland limit). Changing the border of an open bank on Wayland reopens its window (`BankWindow::needs_rebuild`). On X11 nothing changes. 4 unit tests (monitor of a point, margins, centering, clamped move). NOT checked by eye.
- Feature (Wayland, step 2 of `docs/WAYLAND-PORT.md`): on a Wayland compositor with `wlr-layer-shell` (labwc, sway, KDE) the desktop parts of OpusDM are layer surfaces. New `opusdm_ui::layer` (`LayerSpec`, `Anchors`, `Margins`, `Exclusive`, `init` before realize, `update` live, `is_layer`, `available`) on `gtk4-layer-shell` 0.7 (needs `libgtk4-layer-shell` at build and run time, on X11 too). Desktop window of each monitor: BACKGROUND layer, all edges, ignores exclusive zones, keyboard on demand (`desktop::layer_spec`). Panel bars: TOP layer across their edge with an automatic exclusive zone in place of the struts, moved live by `make_panel_window` (`window::layer_spec`). Dock: BOTTOM layer on its edge with `EDGE_MARGIN`, centered by the compositor in the space the panel leaves free (`window::layer_spec`). Run window: OVERLAY at the top of the focused monitor, exclusive keyboard. Device popup: OVERLAY in the corner next to the panel. On X11 `init` does nothing and every X11 path is unchanged; the "window is not on X11" lines became debug traces. 1 unit test. Checked: in a headless labwc (`labwc -d`) three layer surfaces are created at hub start (desktop and panel interactive on demand, dock not interactive). NOT checked by eye, NOT checked under X11 after the change (no Xvfb here).
- Feature (Wayland, step 1 of `docs/WAYLAND-PORT.md`): OpusDM picks X11 or Wayland at run time, one binary for both. New `opusdm_ipc::session` (`WindowSystem`, `detect_from`, `current`, `is_x11`, `is_wayland`): `GDK_BACKEND` naming x11/wayland wins, then `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, then `DISPLAY`, the order GDK uses. Under a Wayland session `DISPLAY` usually names XWayland, so every X11 entry point now checks the session instead of trusting that an X connection opens: `opusdm_x11::connect` (and so `with_x11`) refuses outside X11, and the hub hot plug, screen size watcher, EWMH observer, XEmbed tray, global key grabs, launch focus, Start in Tray, virtual screen count and switch, and the snap watcher do nothing on Wayland. On X11 nothing changes. New `run-wayland.sh` (nested labwc, `--headless` for scripted tests) and `labwc-rc.xml` (the sandbox config, counterpart of `openbox-rc.xml`); `run-x11.sh` now unsets `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` and sets `GDK_BACKEND=x11`, since from a Wayland desktop GTK would otherwise open on the host instead of Xephyr. 5 unit tests. Checked: the workspace builds, the hub starts in a headless labwc and stays up (panel, dock and desktop are plain windows for now). NOT checked by eye (no screenshot tool yet), NOT re-checked under Xephyr.
- Feature (terminal): new **Theme** tab in Preferences (new `crates/terminal/src/theme_editor.rs`). The theme selector moved there from General; below it `ColorDialogButton`s for text, background, cursor, cursor text, selection and selected text (the last four with a **Default** check box, meaning "left to VTE") and the 16 palette colours. Edits are previewed live (`Shared::preview_with`). **Reset** reloads the selected theme; **Export as New Theme...** asks a name (default "<theme> (custom)"), asks before replacing an existing file, writes `terminal-themes/<slug>.json` (`theme::save_user_theme`, `theme_file_name`, `to_hex`) and selects the new theme. Save with unexported colour edits offers Export... or Discard. 4 new unit tests (29 in total). Checked by eye: the Theme tab shows the colours of the current theme. NOT checked in the session: editing a colour and the export dialog (the automated clicks were stopped because a window of the user covered the dialog).
- Fix (terminal): picking another theme (or font, cursor, scrollback...) in Preferences showed nothing until **Save**, so it looked like it did not work. The General options are now previewed live on every open terminal (`Shared::preview`, connected to every General widget); Save keeps them, Cancel or closing the dialog re-applies the saved config. Checked in the running session with a scratch config: Dracula shown at once, Cancel back to OpusDM Dark, `terminal.json` untouched.
- Feature (hub): at every start the hub makes `opusdm-lister.desktop` the default application for `inode/directory` (new `crates/hub/src/folder_handler.rs`, GIO `set_as_default_for_type`, which writes `~/.config/mimeapps.list` like `xdg-mime default`), so `xdg-open` on a folder, file choosers and other programs open a Lister instead of Dolphin. Nothing is written when it already is the default; a missing desktop entry (development build never installed) is logged and skipped. Note that `~/.config/mimeapps.list` is shared with other desktop sessions (KDE too, unless its own `kde-mimeapps.list` overrides it). 2 unit tests; checked with a scratch `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`: the first call switched Dolphin to the Lister entry, the second wrote nothing, `xdg-mime query default inode/directory` then reports `opusdm-lister.desktop`. NOT checked with the real hub restarted. The install message of `build-release.sh` says so instead of suggesting `xdg-mime`.
- Fix (terminal): the **Themes Folder** button of Preferences opened the folder with the system default for `inode/directory` (`gtk::FileLauncher`), which is Dolphin on a KDE install. It now asks the hub for a Lister (`ToHub::SpawnLister` over the hub socket, as `opusdm-settings` does for the button banks); only when the hub cannot be reached (logged) does it fall back to the default application. Checked in the running session: a Lister opens on `~/.config/opusdm/terminal-themes`.
- Change (hub): **Open Terminal Here** in the desktop context menu opens `opusdm-terminal -d <Desktop folder>` (looked up next to the hub, else on `PATH`). Only when it cannot start (logged) does it fall back to `$TERMINAL` and the common terminals as before. `OpusDM-Features-Guide.md` updated (also the Lister **External** button, no longer xterm). Builds, hub tests pass; NOT checked in the running session (needs the hub restarted with `live-update.sh`).
- Change (terminal): in the right-click menu **Preferences...** is a section of its own, with a separator line above and below. Checked by eye in the running session.
- Feature (terminal): the Preferences dialog has two tabs, **General** (appearance, scrolling) and **Hotkeys**. Each hotkey row has a **Grab...** button that opens a "Press a key combination" window like the Lister hotkey editor: the pressed keys are written as a GTK accelerator (`<Control><Alt>v`), Escape cancels, modifier-only presses are ignored and so are printable keys without Ctrl/Alt/Super (they would steal typing from the shell). Save now refuses two actions on the same keys and compares hotkeys with the defaults by key, not by text, so a grabbed default is not stored as a change. Checked in the running session with synthetic input: plain `a` ignored, Ctrl+Alt+V grabbed, both tabs shown.
- Feature (terminal): new program `opusdm-terminal` (new crate `crates/terminal`), a VTE terminal inspired by Terminator and Konsole. Tabs (`gtk::Notebook`, tab bar from the second tab on) holding a tree of `gtk::Paned` splits: Split Vertically `Ctrl+Shift+E` (side by side), Split Horizontally `Ctrl+Shift+O` (one above the other), close pane `Ctrl+Shift+W` or shell exit (the sibling takes its place), focus by direction `Alt+Arrows` or cycle `Ctrl+Shift+N/P`; a new pane or tab starts in the directory of the current shell (OSC 7, else `/proc/<foreground pgrp>/cwd`). Every hotkey is configurable (22 actions, GTK accelerator syntax, Terminator defaults). Files dropped from a Lister or the desktop are typed at the prompt as shell-quoted paths (`quote.rs`). Link recognition: URL and e-mail regexes plus OSC 8 hyperlinks, Ctrl+click opens them, the right-click menu has Open/Copy Link. Export Contents (`Ctrl+Shift+S`) writes the whole scrollback to a text file. Preferences dialog: theme, font (`FontDialogButton`), cursor, bold bright, bell, scrollback (default 2000, 0 = unlimited, VTE -1) and hotkeys, saved in `~/.config/opusdm/terminal.json` and applied to every open terminal. Terminal themes are separate from the Lister ones: 10 built-in presets plus user JSON themes in `~/.config/opusdm/terminal-themes/`. CLI: `-d DIR`, `-e COMMAND`, `-x PROGRAM ARGS...`. 25 unit tests (config, themes, quoting, links, CLI, actions, neighbour pane picking). Checked in the running session with synthetic input: splits both ways, tabs, pane close on `exit`, right-click menu with link detection, export, and a real drag of `my file.txt` from a Lister (typed as `'.../my file.txt' `). The Preferences dialog opens with the current values; saving it and Ctrl+click on a link are NOT checked by eye.
- Feature (iconview): on the desktop (Free layout) **Ctrl+drag** now starts a file drag (`text/uri-list`) to other windows instead of moving the icons; the icon under the pointer is always part of the drag even if the Ctrl+press unselected it. A plain drag still repositions. Unit test updated. Builds; NOT checked by eye (needs the hub rebuilt).
- Change (ipc): the default global hotkey `Ctrl+\` runs `exec:opusdm-terminal` instead of `exec:terminator` (only for a new `hotkeys.json`).
- Change (lister): the **External** button of the terminal drawer opens `opusdm-terminal -d <folder>` instead of `xterm`, and logs a failure instead of ignoring it.
- Change (scripts): `build-release.sh` ships `opusdm-terminal`; `live-update.sh --all` does not kill it on purpose (it would end the user's shells, maybe the one running the script).
- Feature (ipc): three more default global hotkeys: `Ctrl+\` runs `exec:terminator`, `Ctrl+Print` runs `exec:opusdm-shot`, `Ctrl+Shift+Space` runs `exec:opusdm-run`. They are written only on first run (when `hotkeys.json` does not exist yet), so an existing setup is not changed.

## 2026-10-09
- Fix (shot): launching `opusdm-shot` (hotkey `exec:opusdm-shot`) while it was already open did nothing. The second launch now closes the open overlay or editor windows and starts a new screenshot; the capture waits 250 ms (`RESTART_SETTLE_MS`) so the closed windows are off screen. Builds; NOT checked by eye in a running session.
- Fix (hub): a program started by an `exec:` hotkey (for example `Ctrl+\` -> `konsole`) or a Run step opened its window behind the current one. Openbox refuses the focus to a window it cannot tie to a recent user action, and the key press went to the hub's global grab; Konsole made it worse, since all its windows live in one process with an old `_NET_WM_USER_TIME`. New `crates/hub/src/launch_focus.rs`: before a detached Run step starts (not with `window` or `wait`), the hub announces the launch with a startup notification `new:` message carrying the current X server time and passes the same id in `DESKTOP_STARTUP_ID`. As a fallback, a thread watches `_NET_CLIENT_LIST` for 5 s: the first new window with the same startup id, the same pid or a `WM_CLASS` equal to the program name gets a `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` request with the pager source when it has not got the focus within 300 ms; then a `remove:` message ends the sequence. Nothing changes without an X connection. 5 unit tests. Checked on the real session with a temporary example binary: with Brave just used (a fake key press through XTEST), a plain `konsole` launch stays behind Brave, the same launch through `launch_focus` gets the focus. Confirmed by the user with the hub restarted by `live-update.sh`: `Ctrl+\` now opens Konsole in front.
- Feature (lister): **Ctrl+double click** on a folder (details and icon view) and **Ctrl+Return** on the folder under the cursor open that folder in a new Lister (`ToHub::SpawnLister`), also inside archives and on remote sites; on a file both behave like a plain double click / Return. A capture-phase `EventControllerLegacy` on the window records whether the last button press held Ctrl (`Ui::ctrl_click`, reset by any key press), `activate_row` reads it; Ctrl+Return resolves to the new `Action::EnterNew` over the list only (not in text fields). 1 unit test in `keys.rs`. Builds and tests pass; NOT checked by eye in a running session.

## 2026-10-08
- Change (settings): the tool buttons moved from the row above the tabs into two new tabs: **Advanced** (Hotkeys, File Types, Manage Functions, Button Banks, Apps) and **System** (Input, Screens). The Startup Apps section moved from the Desktop tab to its own **Startup Apps** tab. Builds; NOT checked by eye.
- Change (hub): **Manage Functions** is gone from the **Desktop** submenu of the desktop menu (it is a button of OpusDM Settings); removed `launch_functions_manager` and `spawn_lister_tool`.
- Change (settings): two more buttons in the tool row of OpusDM Settings: **Manage Functions** (`opusdm-lister --manage-functions`) and **Button Banks** (sends `ToHub::OpenBankManager` to the running hub, which opens the manager, since the banks live in the hub). The row is now a wrapping `FlowBox` (4 per line). The menu entries stay. Builds; NOT checked by eye.
- Change (settings, hub): OpusDM Settings has a row of buttons above its tabs: **Hotkeys**, **File Types**, **Apps**, **Input** and **Screens**. Each starts its tool as its own process (`opusdm-lister --edit-hotkeys`, `opusdm-lister --edit-filetypes`, `opusdm-apps-editor`, `opusdm-input-config`, `opusdm-screen`), looked up next to the Settings binary, then on `PATH` (`crates/theme-config/src/tools.rs`). The same five entries are gone from the **Desktop** submenu of the desktop context menu, which keeps Rearrange icons, Manage Functions, Edit Theme and Settings; `launch_hotkey_editor`, `launch_filetypes_editor` and `launch_tool` were removed from the hub. `docs/usage.md` points to the buttons. Builds; NOT checked by eye in a running session.
- Fix (scripts): `live-update.sh --all` now also stops `opusdm-input-config` and `opusdm-screen`, and really stops `opusdm-apps-editor`. `pkill -x` compares against the kernel process name, which keeps only 15 characters, so a longer name never matched (`pgrep` even says so); the names are now cut to 15 characters before the match (`opusdm-input-co`, `opusdm-apps-edi`). Checked with a process named `opusdm-input-config`: its kernel name is `opusdm-input-co` and the cut name stops it.
- Change (scripts): `live-update.sh` no longer sends the output of the hub it restarts to `/dev/null`. It goes to `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/opusdm/hub.log`, the previous run's log is kept as `hub.log.1`, and the hub runs with `RUST_BACKTRACE=1`, so a panic leaves its message and stack (the monitor-off crash left nothing to read).
- Fix (hub): turning a monitor off or unplugging it while the hub runs (for example **Only HDMI-1** in Screens) aborted the hub and left a black desktop. When the monitor count changes, `full_rebuild_desktop_windows` closed every desktop window with `close()` while it still held `widgets.windows` mutably; `close()` runs the window's close-request handler (`request_quit`), which reads `widgets.windows` through `primary_window()`: a `RefCell already mutably borrowed` panic inside a GTK callback, so an abort with nothing in the log (the hub started by `live-update.sh` writes to `/dev/null`). The old windows are now taken out of the `RefCell` first and removed with `destroy()`, which does not ask the close-request handler (that handler is for the window manager closing the desktop, and it would also have kept the window open behind a "Quit OpusDM?" question). Found with a backtrace under gdb on the real laptop and HDMI monitor; checked the same way: the hub stays up when eDP-1 is turned off and when it comes back.
- Fix (screen): the Screens window froze when a resolution was picked from the drop down (the open list stayed on screen, the window stopped answering). Picking a value refilled the panel, and that replaced the model of the very drop down that was handling the selection. `set_items` now replaces a model only when its entries differ and sets the selection only when it moves. Reproduced and checked under Xephyr in demo mode: Resolution and Rotation now apply and the list closes.
- Feature (screen, hub): **screen manager**, a new `opusdm-screen` binary (crate `crates/screen`), like the Display Configuration of KDE Plasma. A canvas with one draggable rectangle per monitor (snaps to the edges of the others, 12 canvas pixels), a panel for the selected monitor (enabled, resolution, refresh rate, rotation, `xrandr --scale`, primary) and the **Extend**, **Duplicate** and **Only NAME** buttons. **Apply** sends one `xrandr` call and asks "Keep these display settings?" with a 15 s countdown; Keep, Revert, the countdown or closing the window decide, and a layout is moved so the top left monitor sits at 0,0. **Remember for these monitors** saves the layout in `~/.config/opusdm/screens.json`, keyed by the sorted EDID ids (bytes 8..16, the output name when there is no EDID), so the same monitors on other ports are found (`Layout::remapped_to`). The library has no GTK code: `model` (`Layout::validate`, `is_mirrored`, `shifted_to_origin`), `parse` (`xrandr --query --props`, the scale is read from the area the output takes against the starred mode), `commands` (one argv; `--scale` only when the monitor is or was scaled, because Xephyr refuses a transform; a rate of 0 is not sent), `modes` (`extend`, `mirror`, `only`), `geometry` (edge snapping and the canvas view), `backend` (`ScreenBackend`, `XrandrBackend`, `session_problem`), `fake`, `store`, `session` (edit, apply, confirm, revert) and `reapply`. The hub starts `screen_hotplug.rs`: it applies the saved layout at start and again after a burst of RandR `ScreenChangeNotify` events (0.5 s settle time), only when the set of connected monitors changed (`reapply_if_changed`), so a layout you set by hand with the same monitors is never undone; no profile means the screens are left alone. `opusdm-screen --demo` runs on a fake backend. New **Screens** button in the Desktop submenu of the desktop menu; `build-release.sh` ships the binary. Tests: parser on captured output, argv generation, modes, snapping and view, profiles, the session with the fake backend, the reapply logic. Checked by hand: the parser and `Session` on the real laptop and HDMI monitor (read only), the window under Xephyr in demo mode (drag with snap, Duplicate, Apply, the countdown going back by itself), the real `xrandr` backend apply and revert against Xephyr (resolution change), and the hub applying a saved profile at start under Xephyr and not undoing a manual mode change. NOT checked: Apply on the real monitors, hot plug of a real monitor, rotation and scale on real hardware, Wayland. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-screen-manager-design.md`; usage in section 3.34 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Change (hub, Startup Apps "Tray"): the window of a "start in tray" app is no longer made transparent and moved past the bottom right corner of the screen. It now opens normally, where the app puts it; once visible (plus `SETTLE_TIME`, 300 ms) the hub minimizes it (ICCCM `WM_CHANGE_STATE` with `IconicState`) and sends `WM_DELETE_WINDOW`, up to 3 times, until the window leaves `_NET_CLIENT_LIST` (the app hid it) or is gone; a window still visible before a try is minimized again, one that will not close stays minimized. The window is never moved, so it keeps its real place. Toolkits remember the minimize: measured with a PyQt6 app, a plain `show()` from the tray mapped it minimized again (`WM_STATE` Iconic). So the window's `_NET_WM_STATE` is read before the minimize and, once the app has hidden it (Openbox erases the property then), written back without `_NET_WM_STATE_HIDDEN`; Qt and GTK re-read it and drop the minimized state, and a maximize is kept. Removed `OFF_SCREEN_GAP`, `move_off_screen`, `move_back`, `request_move` and the opacity property. Pure `iconify_data` and `without_state`, 2 unit tests. Tooltip of the Tray checkbox and the `start_in_tray` doc updated. Checked live in Xephyr with Openbox: a GTK3 app (window at 300,200 with a `StatusIcon`), a PyQt6 app (same, `QSystemTrayIcon` over SNI) and a PyQt6 app started maximized all went to the tray within a second, and when shown again (GTK `present()`, Qt plain `show()`) came back at 300,200 in the normal state, or maximized for the third. NOT checked: Telegram and Enpass themselves.
- Fix (panel, SNI tray): every SNI icon (Telegram, Enpass, MEGAsync...) disappeared for good after the bars were rebuilt. The bars are rebuilt on every monitor geometry change and every root window resize (`watch_monitors`, `watch_screen_size`), which can happen at login while the screen settles; the SNI event receiver was handed to the first primary bar only (a known gap noted in the code), so every later primary bar had no SNI strip, and the events kept going to the strip of the closed window. The hosted items now live in `PanelState::sni_items`, kept by a single drain in `attach` (like `tray_order` for XEmbed) through the pure `sni::apply_to_list`; `widgets::sni::SniStrip` replaces `build`, and each primary bar gets a new strip seeded with every item already hosted. 1 unit test (order kept, update in place, removal, `Changed` for an unknown id).

- Fix (hub, settings): pressing Apply in Settings no longer refreshes the whole desktop when nothing it controls changed. Every Apply used to redo all of it: rewrite and reload the compositor (`SIGUSR1` to picom), resize the icon field, re-send the panel and dock layout, and re-set the GTK theme (GTK restyles every window on a property write even for an unchanged value). New pure `opusdm_hub::desktop_diff::diff` tells which group changed (transparency, virtual screens, snap, icon size, grid, panel edge, dock layout, desktop folder) and `apply_event` redoes only those; with nothing changed it does nothing. `apply_theme_mode` writes the GTK theme name and the dark preference only when they differ. The server drops a `SetSettings` / `SetDesktopSettings` equal to the current value: no save, no message to the Listers, no event to the GTK side. `background::install` already skipped an identical background; it now logs (debug) when it does restart. Tests: 7 for `desktop_diff`, 1 server test for an identical apply emitting no event.

- Fix (panel, XEmbed tray): GTK3 tray icons were drawn small in the top left corner of their square. Measured in Xephyr: the container was 24x24 and the manager announced `_NET_SYSTEM_TRAY_ICON_SIZE` 24, but the icon window was 16x16 at 0,0: the tray sized it to 24 when docking and GTK then resized it back to its own request, which nothing undid. The tray now handles the icon's `ConfigureNotify` and a resize of its container: an icon that does not fill its container is resized to fill it, and after `MAX_FORCED_RESIZES` (5) such resizes, for a client that keeps resizing back, it keeps its size and is centred instead, so the two can't resize each other forever. Docked icons are tracked with their container size and the forced count (`Docked`). Pure `icon_fit` with 3 unit tests (grow, leave alone, centre after the limit, larger icons cropped evenly).
