Checked live: two GTK3 `StatusIcon`s are 24x24 inside their 24x24 containers and drawn full size and centred; GTK took the new size at the first request.

- Fix (panel, SNI tray): clicking a tray icon whose app is busy (MEGAsync, about 25 s at its start) no longer freezes the panel. The DBusMenu was read on the GTK thread, on a new bus connection at every click, with `AboutToShow` and `GetLayout` blocking and no timeout; the click on a menu entry (`Event`) blocked too. They are now `SniCmd::FetchMenu` (the tree comes back on a channel) and `SniCmd::MenuClicked`, made on the SNI side over its shared connection with the 30 s call timeout, and the command loop runs every command on its own thread, so a busy app no longer delays clicks on the others (`Activate`/`ContextMenu` included). The click opens a small popover at once ("Waiting for the app...") that turns into the menu when the app answers, says "The app did not send its menu" when it fails, and drops a reply that comes after the user closed it. Popovers are unparented once closed (one stayed attached to the button per click). Checked live in Xephyr with a test item whose `GetLayout` answers after 8 s: 1.2 s after the click the panel showed the waiting popover (so it kept drawing), at 10 s the menu with its two entries, and clicking an entry reached the app as `Event 1 clicked`.

- Change (hub, panel): the Startup Apps' wait times count from the moment the panel's tray exists, not from the hub start. The apps were launched before the GTK application (and so the panel) started: an app with a wait of 0 found no tray, so a "Tray" app could quit when its window was closed and an SNI app could register with no watcher and never show. New `opusdm_panel::tray_ready`: the XEmbed thread reports once it owns `_NET_SYSTEM_TRAY_S<n>` and has announced it, the SNI thread once it owns the watcher name or has joined an existing watcher; a thread that cannot start reports too, so nobody waits for a tray that will never come. `startup_apps::start` waits for both on a thread of its own, at most `TRAY_WAIT_LIMIT` (20 s, then it starts anyway and logs it). The section hint and the `wait_secs` doc say so. Tests: 3 for `tray_ready` (both trays needed, no wait when ready, limit), 1 for the apps waiting for the ready signal; the old start tests go through `start_after` with an immediate signal. Checked live in Xephyr with three apps at wait 0: the "Tray" test app started hidden with its tray icon, and both test SNI items showed (before this change they never did, they registered before the watcher existed).

- Fix (tests): the GTK tests of `opusdm-desktop-config` failed now and then (`assertion failed: !ptr.is_null()` in a widget constructor). libtest runs every `#[test]` on its own thread and GTK may only be used from one: the 6 GTK tests all called `gtk::init` at the same moment, a race the `gtk` crate does not catch, and then used GTK from 6 threads at once. With `--test-threads=1` the race was gone and 5 of the 6 panicked with "Attempted to initialize GTK from two different threads". New test-only `gtk_test::run`: one thread initializes GTK and runs every test body in turn, a failed assertion is raised again in the calling test, and with no display the bodies are skipped as before. After the change: 0 failures in 60 parallel runs, all pass with `--test-threads=1`, and a deliberately wrong assertion still fails its test.

- Feature (settings, hub): the Lister the hub opened on the home folder at every start (hard coded in `hub/src/main.rs` since the first hub commit) can be turned off. New `DesktopSettings::open_lister_at_start` (default true, so old `desktop.json` files keep the old behaviour) and a checkbox "Open a Lister on the home folder at start" at the top of Settings / Desktop / Startup Apps; read once when the hub starts. Unit tests: default, old files, false read back; the section reports the seeded choice. Checked live in Xephyr: off, no Lister process and no Lister window after 6 s; on, one of each. A Lister can still be opened by hand and by scripts. Seen while testing: a GTK test of `opusdm-desktop-config` failed once in about 46 runs, with and without this change; fixed below.

- Change (panel, tray): tray icons closer together. SNI buttons have their own class `opusdm-panel-tray-item` with 2 px horizontal padding (they had the 8 px of every panel button) and both strips space their icons by `tray::ICON_SPACING` (2 px, was 4). Checked in Xephyr: the step between two SNI icons went from about 44 to 32 px; what is left is the button border.
- Feature (settings, hub): Startup Apps can start "in tray". Each row of Settings / Desktop / Startup Apps has a **Tray** checkbox (`StartupApp::start_in_tray`, default false, old files load). X11 has no standard way to ask an app to start with no window, so `hub/src/tray_start.rs` watches `_NET_CLIENT_LIST` (watch created before the launch) for up to 30 s and takes the first normal window (no type or `normal`) whose `_NET_WM_PID` is the launched process, one of its descendants or a process of its group (the launch has its own group, so wrapper scripts that fork and exit still match). The window is made fully transparent (`_NET_WM_WINDOW_OPACITY` 0, honoured by picom) and moved past the bottom right corner of the screen at once; once it is visible it gets `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` after 300 ms, again up to 3 times while it stays visible (a GTK3 app under Openbox ignored a close sent as soon as the window was listed in 2 runs out of 3; with the wait and the check, 5 out of 5); then it is made opaque again and put back where it was, so the app shows it in a normal place when it is opened from its tray icon. Only the first window is closed, so a window opened later from the tray is never touched; a window without `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` is left open (the only other way would be to kill the app). An app with no tray icon quits instead: the hint under the section says so. `spawn_detached` now returns the pid. 7 unit tests (`/proc/<pid>/stat` parsing, ancestry and process group, loops, window types). Checked live in Xephyr with Openbox and picom: a GTK3 test app with a window and a tray icon started hidden with only its tray icon, the window was transparent and off screen from the first sample (50 ms), and when the app showed it again it was opaque and on screen. Openbox keeps a few pixels of a window on screen whatever a client asks (here 28 px), so without a compositor that corner can flash briefly. NOT checked: real apps (Telegram, MEGAsync, Discord), other window managers.

- Fix (panel, SNI tray): MEGAsync never showed up in the tray. Measured with `dbus-monitor`: MEGAsync registers at once but answers the first property read only about 25 s later (its startup), right at dbus-daemon's reply timeout. The watcher read the item inside the `RegisterStatusNotifierItem` handler, blocking zbus's executor (every other app registering meanwhile waited too); when the read failed the item was dropped but stayed in the registered list, so the app was never shown and a re-registration was ignored. `sni.rs` is rewritten: the handler only records the item and returns; one worker thread per item reads it with a single `GetAll` (no property-cache proxy), retries after 1, 2, 4, 8 s then every 15 s until the app answers or leaves the bus, and re-reads on change, at most once every 150 ms (MEGAsync sends `NewIcon` every 60 ms while it syncs). Changes now also come from the spec's `NewIcon`/`NewTitle`/`NewStatus`/`NewToolTip`/`NewAttentionIcon`/`NewOverlayIcon`/`NewMenu` signals, which Qt, Electron and libappindicator send instead of `PropertiesChanged` (before, those icons never updated). One dispatcher thread reads every subscribed signal on its own connection (match rules per item: its signals by owner unique name and path, and its name losing its owner) instead of two blocking threads per item that were never stopped. Removed items leave the registry, so an app that restarts with the same name shows again. Items are keyed by bus name plus path (two items of one app no longer collide); `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` publishes that joined form and the watcher emits `StatusNotifierItemRegistered`/`Unregistered`. Calls to apps time out after 30 s (dbus-broker has no timeout of its own). The click loop blocks in `recv` instead of waking 20 times a second. In host-only mode (another watcher owns the name) the panel also registers as a host. 13 unit tests (name and path parsing, `GetAll` decoding, pixmap checks, retry delays, registry lookups and wake-ups).
- Fix (panel, XEmbed tray): docked icons drawn over other icons. The icon windows are placed in absolute coordinates and were moved only when the XEmbed set changed, so when an SNI icon appeared or another widget of the bar changed width the squares moved and the windows did not. `Tray::follow_layout` re-reads the squares after every frame the bar paints (only when something changed, so an idle bar costs nothing) and moves only the windows whose rect changed; `reposition_now` still sends all of them and now also raises them above the panel. The containers had no background, so the parts an icon leaves transparent (GTK3 icons) showed old screen content, which looked like another icon over it: they now have the panel colour (`window::PANEL_BACKGROUND`, also used by the CSS). A repeated dock request for an already docked icon is ignored (it used to leave a second container mapped at the old place). The icon is reparented before its events are selected, so the unmap caused by reparenting a mapped icon no longer undocks it at once; the selection is a checked request, so an icon that died before docking no longer leaves a container behind. Icons are put in the save-set, so a panel crash or restart gives them back to the root instead of destroying the apps' windows, and an icon that only unmaps is moved back to the root before its container is destroyed. The manager window sets `_NET_SYSTEM_TRAY_ICON_SIZE`.
- Checked live in Xephyr with a private session bus: a test SNI item busy for 35 s timed out once, was retried and appeared; a second item registered meanwhile appeared at once; both disappeared from the panel and from `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` when their process ended; two GTK3 `StatusIcon` XEmbed icons moved left and back when an SNI icon came and went, with a clean background; the real MEGAsync 6.5.1 appeared after its startup. NOT checked: the panel in the real session (the hub was not restarted), several monitors, a second watcher (host-only mode), Wine or Java XEmbed icons. GTK3 icons drawn at 16 px in the top left corner of their square: fixed below.

## 2026-10-07
- Feature (settings): a brightness slider in Settings / Desktop (section BRIGHTNESS, 10 to 100 percent, applied live while it moves, not stored in `desktop.json`). New crate `opusdm-brightness`: a `Backend` trait with three implementations tried in order by `detect`: `Sysfs` (laptop backlight, `/sys/class/backlight`, needs write access to the file), `Ddc` (`ddcutil getvcp/setvcp 10`, scaled to the max the monitor reports) and `Xrandr` (software gamma of the first connected output, only darkens the picture and is lost when X restarts). When none works the slider stays disabled and the text lists why each was skipped (for example the package to install). `spawn` runs detection and every `set` on a worker thread (`ddcutil` takes up to a second) and collapses a burst of slider values to the last one, so there is no timer debounce. `BrightnessSection` (`desktop-config/src/ui.rs`) holds weak references in its event loop, so closing the window stops the worker. The minimum is 10 percent so the screen cannot be turned black. 18 unit tests (parsers on fixed text, sysfs on a temp dir, detection order, worker with a fake backend). Checked live on this machine with `cargo run -p opusdm-brightness --example probe [xrandr]`: DDC/CI read 54, set 60, restored 54; xrandr read 100, set 60, restored 100. NOT checked: the slider in the real Settings window, the sysfs backend on real hardware (no backlight here), several monitors (only the first connected output is used).
- Change (network): `SecretServiceStore::set` writes the Wi-Fi key to the `login` keyring (`get_collection_by_alias("login")`, no fallback: it fails with a keyring error instead of writing to a weaker place). The default keyring of this machine is a text file with plain `secret=` lines, only `login.keyring` is the encrypted binary format opened by the login, so the previous entry did NOT encrypt the key at rest. Lookups and deletes still search every keyring, so a key kept elsewhere is still found. The key of os3-5g that had already been typed in the panel (it connected: the real flow from password window to connected worked) was moved from the default keyring to `login` once, by a throwaway program that was not kept; the secret was only in memory. Not covered by a test (needs a live keyring).
- Fix (system, not in the repo): why `~/.local/share/keyrings` filled with `Default_N.keyring`. `Default.keyring` and `Default_keyring.keyring` each held an item `com.example.test/FlutterSecureStorage` whose secret is a JSON on several lines; the text keyring format cannot hold that, so gnome-keyring logged "keyring was in an invalid or unrecognized format" at every start and dropped the whole file (62 and 189 sections, all other items unreadable). Fixed by a script that removes only the multi line items (`Default.keyring` 13 items kept, `Default_keyring.keyring` 45 kept; those two items are only in the backup), checked with a strict GLib `KeyFile` parse and a second gnome-keyring on a private bus (no error, 13 and 45 items). The 10 empty collections (`Default_1..5`, `7..11`, 92 bytes, header only) were moved out. Backup of the whole directory in `~/keyrings-backup-2026-10-07` (mode 700, plain secrets in it, delete when no longer needed). NOT done: the Flutter test app still writes there and will break the file again; `Default_6` (Zed, TablePlus), `Default_12` (Brave) and `Default_keyring_1` (TablePlus) were left as they are; the running daemon loads the repaired files only at its next start, the 10 removed collections stay listed until then. The cause of the 12 empty collections is NOT proven (most likely an app that finds no loadable default and creates one).
- Change (network): the Wi-Fi keys are no longer written in the NetworkManager profile (a plain text file in `/etc/NetworkManager/system-connections/`, the way the previous fix of this day did it); they live in the desktop keyring. `NmBackend::register_agent` (called by the watcher once NetworkManager answers; a failure is only logged) serves a NetworkManager secret agent, `agent.rs`, registered as `org.opusdm.network` at `AgentManager.Register`. It answers `GetSecrets` from the keyring, keeps the new key of a profile on `SaveSecrets`, forgets it on `DeleteSecrets`, and only serves calls whose sender is the owner of `org.freedesktop.NetworkManager` (any other process on the bus gets `NotAuthorized`, or it could read the keys). When NetworkManager asks for a new key (`REQUEST_NEW`, the one it had was refused) the agent deletes the kept one and answers `NoSecrets`, so the next click asks for the password again instead of retrying a wrong one for ever. `keystore.rs` has the `KeyStore` trait, `SecretServiceStore` (crate `secret-service` 5.2, blocking, DH encrypted session, connects at every call so a keyring that starts later just works) and `FakeKeyStore` for tests; the items use the attributes of `nm-applet` and GNOME (`xdg:schema org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.Connection`, `connection-uuid`, `setting-name`, `setting-key`), so those tools find them. `connect` on a saved profile whose key is agent owned or not saved: key in the keyring, go on; no key and no password, `password_needed` (the panel asks, as before); a password, it goes into the keyring and is forgotten again if the connection fails. New networks are added with `psk-flags` 1 so NetworkManager hands the key to the agent (`SaveSecrets`) instead of writing it to disk. The profile of NetworkManager is never changed now (`with_stored_key` and `restore_profile` are gone). 12 new tests (agent answers, refused key forgotten, sender rule is NOT unit tested, keystore attributes and label, flags). Checked live: the keyring round trip, the registration, and a throwaway agent owned profile for os3-5g with a wrong key: NetworkManager called `GetSecrets` (flags 1 several times, then 5 and 7), the agent served the key and then dropped it on `REQUEST_NEW` and the keyring was empty after. NOT checked: a correct key (no Wi-Fi password here), the `SaveSecrets` path of a new network, the password window in the real panel, a locked keyring (the unlock prompt). If the keyring is missing, a new network fails with "wrong password?" and a line in stderr, and a saved one asks for the password at every click.
- Fix (network, panel): joining a saved Wi-Fi network no longer always ends in "Could not connect". The profiles made by other tools keep the key out of the profile (`psk-flags` agent-owned or not saved) and NetworkManager asked a secret agent that does not exist ("no secrets: No agents were available"). `NmBackend::connect` now reads the profile first: if its key is not stored and there is no password it returns `NetError::password_needed()` (new `needs_password` flag) without trying to activate; the panel opens the existing password window and sends the connect again with it. With a password the backend stores it in the profile (`Update`, `psk-flags` 0) and activates; if that fails the original settings are put back, so a wrong password does not stay, and the error says "wrong password?". Pure parts tested (`key_not_stored`, `with_stored_key`, error flag, worker report order). Checked live against the real NetworkManager only up to the password request (os3-5g and os3: asked for a password, no activation started); the store and activate step was NOT run for lack of the Wi-Fi password. The generic "Could not connect" text is unchanged for other failures.
- Docs: `docs/YOUTUBE.md`, a scene by scene flow for a YouTube demo video (demo data, script, timings, titles, 60 second cut).
- Docs: `docs/FEATURES.md`, a marketing style feature list (taglines, section copy, comparison table, site structure) to feed the promotional website.

- Feature (snap): the zone preview has rounded corners (radius 10 px, `CORNER_RADIUS`). The window is clipped with the X Shape bounding region, rebuilt only when the size changes; the pure `rounded_spans` makes it from one rectangle per run of rows with the same inset (tests: cut corners, no overlap and the area of a rounded rectangle, symmetry, radius limited to half the shorter side, empty sizes). The border is now the window background in the border colour and the fill is painted over the inside with its own rounded region, so the ring follows the corners (before, 4 border rectangles). `Overlay::create(display, require_compositor)` makes a preview without a compositor, for tests: a new ignored live test checks the Shape rectangles of a real preview window and its pixels (border colour on the edge, fill inside). The Shape region has hard edges, so the corners are not antialiased. NOT seen with a compositor (picom is not installed here).
- Change (snap): the watcher no longer wakes 20 times a second when idle. `Display` selects the XInput 2 raw button press and release events of every pointer on the root window and `Display::wait_for_button` sleeps in `poll(2)` on the connection until one arrives (events already queued by a reply are drained first, they would never wake the poll) or 500 ms pass, which only bounds how late a switch of the setting is noticed. A press wakes it in under a millisecond (measured under Xephyr) and it then polls every 16 ms until the button is up, as before. When the server has no XInput 2 it logs one line and polls every 50 ms as it did. The window type and size hints were already cached for the last window checked, so a drag does not read them again. NOT reduced: while a button is down a sample still costs the pointer query, the active window read and the frame geometry (3 round trips, the last one pipelined). Added an ignored live test for the wake up.
- Change (snap): the restore of a snapped window no longer sleeps 120 ms on the watcher thread. `Display::restore` is split in `begin_restore` (unmaximize, cancel Openbox's move with Escape) and `finish_restore` (resize, start the new move); the watcher keeps a `PendingRestore` and finishes it once `CANCEL_DRAG_WAIT` has passed, so the preview, the state machine and the settle check keep running meanwhile. `watcher::apply` takes a small `Context` (display, overlay, environment, pointer, time, pending work) instead of 7 parameters. The live test passes 17 times in a row after the change; it failed twice in about 25 runs (once before this change) when the machine was busy compiling, a window ended half way through a drag without snapping; the cause was not found.
- Fix (snap): the snap area of a monitor now comes from the struts of the docks, not from `_NET_WORKAREA`. That property is the union over all monitors, so a top panel on one monitor also shrank the zones of the others. New pure module `snap/src/workarea.rs` (`Strut::parse` for `_NET_WM_STRUT_PARTIAL` and the old `_NET_WM_STRUT`, `monitor_area`): each reserved band cuts only the monitors it touches, on the side that faces it, with the screen edges as reference. `Display::environment` reads the struts of every window of `_NET_CLIENT_LIST` (requests pipelined) when a drag may start. With no strut at all it falls back to the old rule (monitor intersected with `_NET_WORKAREA`), so the margins Openbox itself is configured with still count; with struts they do not. Tests: 9 for the strut maths (panel on one of two monitors, both, top plus bottom, left and right ranges, the 4 number form, bad input) and one for `area_for`; the live test now maps a dock with a bottom strut and checks that the zones stop above it. NOT checked with two real monitors (Xephyr has one).
- Test (snap): the live check is now repeatable. `crates/snap/tests/live.rs` (`#[ignore]`) starts the real watcher and drives a window of its own through Openbox with XTEST pointer events: the 7 zones (maximize, the 4 corners, left and right), the rectangle the window really gets, a drop on the bottom edge (no zone), the restore after dragging a snapped window away (size and pointer inside the window) and the first size being kept over two snap and restore cycles; when a compositor runs it also looks for the preview window at the zone rectangle while armed and for its removal after the release. `scripts/snap-live-test.sh [display] [resolution]` starts Xephyr, Openbox and, when installed, picom, runs it and stops everything. Passes 9 times in a row here; picom is NOT installed on this machine, so the preview part of the test has never run (its window lookup is untested).
- Refactor (x11, ui, snap, panel, hub, shot): the shared X11 plumbing is one GTK-free library crate, `crates/x11` (`opusdm-x11`). It holds what used to be `ui/src/x11.rs` (the lazily opened shared connection `X11`, `with_x11`, the atom cache, the EWMH helpers) plus what `snap` and `panel` each had their own copy of: `connect()` (a `Session` of connection, screen and root), `send_to_root`, `Extents` and `extents_of` (`_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS`), `gone`, and the protocol constants (`MOVERESIZE_ALL`, `NET_WM_STATE_ADD` and `REMOVE`, `SOURCE_APPLICATION`, `ALL_DESKTOPS`). `opusdm-ui` re-exports it, so no caller of `opusdm_ui::X11` changed, and keeps only `retry_after_map` (needs the GTK loop). `snap/x11.rs`, `ui/screen.rs`, `hub/global_keys.rs`, `panel/ewmh.rs`, `panel/systray.rs` and `shot/capture.rs` connect through `opusdm_x11::connect()` (7 own `x11rb::connect(None)` calls became one). The ignored live test moved with the code and passes under Xephyr. Not merged: each crate keeps its own `atom_manager!` list (the sets differ), and the 2 other connection sites (`dock`, `buttonbank`) already go through `opusdm-ui`.
- Refactor (hub, lister): the five very long GTK builder functions are split. `open_command_palette` (178 lines): `build_command_row`, `connect_filter`, `make_execute_selected`, `connect_navigation_keys`, `move_selection` and the pure `neighbour_index`. `open_compare_dialog` (184): `build_compare_header`, `connect_compare`, `connect_select_in_lister`, `build_diff_row` and the pure `DiffSummary` and `diff_badge`. `add_column` (239): `setup_cell`, `bind_cell`, `bind_name_cell`, `connect_context_menu`, `connect_drag_source` and `column_sorter`. `open_rules_dialog` (257): `RulesControls` (build, `rules()`, `connect_changed`), `build_preview_row`, `render_preview` and the pure `case_from_index`. `build_desktop_menu` (272): an `add_item` helper (hides the menu, then runs the action) turns each of the 20 blocks into one call, with `add_file_items` and `build_desktop_submenu`. New unit tests for the pure parts. The behaviour was kept; NOT checked by hand in a real session (the dialogs and the desktop menu were not opened).
- Refactor (hub): the tests of `server.rs` (about 1600 lines) and `functions.rs` moved to `server/tests.rs` and `functions/tests.rs` (`server.rs` 2922 to 1295 lines, `functions.rs` 1425 to 748, same 182 tests). `Hub::lister_msg` went from 206 to 125 lines: the long arms became `set_desktop_settings`, `set_bindings`, `set_formats`, `set_functions`, `query_caches` and `register_lister`; the one line arms stay in the match.
- Refactor (watch, devices, audio, network): the three workers share one skeleton in a new library crate `crates/watch` (`opusdm-watch`, no GTK): `run_with_retry` (retry every 30 s, the outage is reported once), `drain_burst` (300 ms coalescing) and `signal_ticks` (the D-Bus signal reader thread). The three `watch.rs` files went from 291 to 148 lines. Behaviour change: devices and network now tell `Outage::recovered` once a session is up, so a second outage is reported again, as audio already did (before, they reported only the first outage of the process). Tests moved to the new crate, with one for the once-per-outage rule. Not merged: the three `backend.rs` (`Backend` plus `FakeBackend`) differ in their trait methods and would need generics for little gain.
- Fix (lister): the list columns no longer `unwrap` GTK downcasts. A factory callback that finds a widget or item of an unexpected type returns without touching the cell, and the column sorter treats an unreadable item as equal, instead of panicking the UI. `lister/archive.rs` had no unwrap left after the mutex change. Left on purpose in `hub/server.rs`: the five `expect` on `thread::Builder::spawn` (accept, command, owner, job and function forwarders); the hub cannot run without them and each already says which thread failed.
- Change (ipc): `menu.json`, `dock.json` and a custom theme's `theme.json` load through `settings::read_json` / `load_json` like the other files, so one place words the errors and a malformed file is now also copied to `<name>.bak` before the next save overwrites it (tests added for menu and dock). Not done: a `ConfigFile<T>` helper for the path plus load plus save trio of the 35 `load_*` functions, they are thin wrappers already and only the hand parsing was duplicated.
- Fix (workspace): `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` now passes (it reported about 40 warnings). `ToHub::SetSettings`, `ToLister::SettingsUpdate`, `HubEvent::ThemeChanged`, `PersistMsg::Settings` and `OutMsg::Welcome` box their big payload (the JSON on the wire is unchanged). Type aliases replace the 13 very complex types (`opusdm_ui::MonitorRect`, `lister::client::Connected`, ...). Five functions with too many arguments take a small struct: `Row::new(name, RowAttrs)`, `lister::build::run(Connected, start)`, `panel::BarServices`, `hub::functions::ExternalCall` and `hub::desktop_windows::DesktopIcons`. `SniEvent` variants lost their `Item` prefix. Test code builds `Default` structs in one expression. Known and NOT fixed: tests that set process-wide env vars (`ipc::sockpath`, `iconview::thumbs::cache`) fail or hang now and then when run in parallel; the same happens on the commit before this work.
- Change (workspace): one version for everything. `version = "0.7.0"` now lives only in `[workspace.package]` of the root `Cargo.toml`; the 26 crates use `version.workspace = true` and `opusdm_ui::VERSION` is `env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")`. A release is a one line edit.
- Cleanup (lister): removed `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `DirDiffItem` and the two fields it hid (`src_size`, `tgt_size`, never read).
- Fix (fsops, lister): cleanup after a failed copy, compress, encrypt, split or archive operation is no longer silent. New `fsops::cleanup::remove_partial` removes a partial file and logs the path when it cannot (a file already gone is ignored); the lister's preview folder and old download folder cleanups log too. Left as they were on purpose: `copy.rs:468` (removes a link before replacing it) and `copy.rs:630` (`remove_dir` that is meant to fail on a non-empty folder).
- Fix (devices, audio, network, panel, hub, lister): a poisoned mutex no longer makes every later call panic. The 6, 6 and 4 `lock().unwrap()` of the backends, the ones in `panel/sni.rs`, `hub/global_keys.rs` and `lister/archive.rs` now use `unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())`, as `ui/x11.rs` already did. Tests of devices, audio, network and panel pass; `hub` and `lister` could NOT be built here (no gstreamer or openssl headers), the change there is the same mechanical replacement.
- Feature (snap): **edge snapping of windows, as in KDE**. Dragging any X11 window (not only OpusDM ones) near a screen edge shows a translucent preview of the zone and releasing the button places the window there: top edge maximizes, the four corners are quarters, the left and right edges are split in thirds (middle = half screen, upper and lower = half width by one third of the height, at the top or at the bottom), the bottom edge between the corners has no zone. Dragging a snapped window away restores its size from before the snap, under the pointer, and snapping again keeps the first size. New library crate `crates/snap` (`opusdm-snap`, no GTK): `zones` (geometry), `restore`, `machine` (the drag state machine, pure), `overlay` (click-through ARGB override-redirect window, fade and slide, needs a compositor), `x11` and `watcher` (a thread that polls the pointer every 16 ms only while the left button is down and reads the active window; reconnects with backoff). A window is considered dragged when its frame moves without changing size, so a click, a rubber band or a resize never snaps. Docks, the desktop, splash and fixed-size windows are ignored. The hub starts the watcher (`opusdm_hub::snap`) and the new **Snap windows dragged to the screen edges** check of the Windows tab of the settings (`DesktopSettings::snap_enabled`, default on, old files load) switches it live. Openbox cannot resize a window in the middle of its own move loop (it reapplies the old size on the next motion, checked live), so the restore cancels the move with an Escape sent through XTEST, resizes the window and starts a new move with `_NET_WM_MOVERESIZE`; the rectangle is shifted by how far the pointer travelled meanwhile. Differences from the design spec: the crate places and restores windows itself instead of asking the hub; the preview is a background and a border of core requests (no rounded corners) so it can slide cheaply; with no RandR or no `_NET_WORKAREA` it falls back to the whole screen instead of disabling snap. Checked live in a nested Xephyr with Openbox and picom, with real drags sent through XTEST: every zone, the preview rectangle while armed and its removal after the release, the bottom edge, and the restore (size and pointer position). NOT checked: a physical mouse (XTEST holds the button as a different device), several monitors, the panel and dock offsets, a session started by `scripts/live-update.sh`.
- Release: version bumped to 0.7.0 (`opusdm_ui::VERSION` shown in the About requester, every crate and `Cargo.lock`).
- Fix (panel): XEmbed tray icons (apps without StatusNotifier) were docked at the top-left corner of the screen, over the application menu, instead of in the tray. `Tray::reposition_now` took each placeholder's bounds relative to the tray box, so the tray's own offset inside the bar was lost (live check: containers at `+0+0` and `+28+0`). Bounds are now taken relative to the bar window. NOT checked live yet: needs `scripts/live-update.sh` and an XEmbed app.
- Feature (network, panel): **network tray**. A button on the right of the panel, after the audio tray, opens a popover like the KDE Networks applet: an **Ethernet** and a **Wi-Fi** switch and the list of Wi-Fi networks (by signal, the connected one first, a lock on protected ones). A click connects (saved and open networks at once, the connected one disconnects, a new protected network asks for the password in a small window and saves the connection). New library crate `crates/network` (`opusdm-network`, no GTK, `zbus` blocking like `devices`): `model`, `parse` (access point properties and flags, hidden names dropped, same name merged, active first then by signal, saved profiles marked), `error`, `backend` (`Backend` trait and `FakeBackend`) and `nm` (NetworkManager: `WirelessEnabled`, `Device.Disconnect` and `ActivateConnection` for Ethernet, `ActivateConnection` or `AddAndActivateConnection` with `wpa-psk` or `sae` for Wi-Fi, waits up to 30 s for the connection and deletes a new profile that failed, `RequestScan`), plus `watch` (coalesces signals for 300 ms and sends a state only when it changed; with no bus or NetworkManager it reports once and retries every 30 s). The password is only in the request and the D-Bus message: `Request` has a `Debug` that hides it and it is never logged. In `opusdm-panel`: `network_model` (pure: icon, tooltip, request coalescing, what a click does), `network_center` (shared state, worker thread so the GTK thread never waits, error line, *Connecting to ...* status) and `widgets/network`. The device, audio and network buttons are now grouped in one box with 2 px between them and a single separator after the group (before, each had its own separator and the gap was about 30 px). Tests: parser and flag rules, ordering and merging, error texts, fake backend, burst coalescing, the pure panel model and the click rules. Checked by hand under Xephyr with this machine's NetworkManager: icon, popover, real network list, the Wi-Fi switch (`nmcli radio wifi` went `disabled` then `enabled`), the password window (Connect greyed while empty, Cancel), the new spacing. NOT checked: switching Ethernet off (it would cut this machine's connection), connecting with a real password (new, saved and wrong), disconnecting, WPA3, the error line for a failed connect. The error of a failed connect shows in the popover line, not in the password window. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-network-tray-design.md` (no separate plan file); usage in section 3.33 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Change (panel): in the audio popover the **Mute** text button is now a speaker icon (with waves when sound is on, crossed when muted), drawn with the same cairo routine as the panel icon; the tooltip still says Mute. NOT checked by hand in the real panel.

## 2026-10-06

- Feature (devices, panel, hub): **device notifier**. A drive icon on the right of the panel, just before the CPU and network monitors, opens a drop-down with the removable volumes: **Mount**, **Open** (new Lister on the mount point), **Unmount** and **Eject** (unmounts every volume of the drive, then ejects and powers off when the drive can). A popup (**Mount**, **Mount and open**, **Ignore**, closes after 10 s or when the device is mounted or unplugged) appears in the corner next to the panel when a device is plugged in. New library crate `crates/devices` (`opusdm-devices`, no GTK, no new dependency): `model`, `filter` (a volume needs a filesystem, no `HintSystem` or `HintIgnore`, and a removable or USB drive), `parse` (udisks2 `GetManagedObjects`, label falls back to the drive name, then to the size), `diff`, `error` (readable texts for `DeviceBusy` and `NotAuthorized`), `backend` (`Backend` trait and `FakeBackend`), `ops` (eject sequence), `udisks` (`zbus` blocking on the system bus; `Mount`, `Unmount`, `Eject`, `PowerOff` with empty options, so udisks2 chooses `/media/<user>/<label>`) and `watch` (a worker that coalesces udisks2 signals for 300 ms, re-lists and diffs; with no bus or udisks2 it reports once and retries every 30 s). GVfs is not installed here, so `gio::VolumeMonitor` was not an option (see the spec). In `opusdm-panel` a `DeviceCenter` owns the shared list (so a monitor-hotplug rebuild keeps the state) and hands one button to the primary bar; `devices_model` is the pure row logic (devices present at start-up are never announced; Eject only on the first volume of a drive). `opusdm_panel::attach` now takes an `on_open_folder` callback; the hub passes one that sends `HubCmd::SpawnRequested`. Tests: parser on built object trees, filter rules, diff, errors, eject sequence and its failure, burst coalescing, the pure panel model, popup placement. Checked by hand under Xephyr with this machine's real USB disks (icon position, the drop-down, **Mount** at `/media/fabio/extreme` with the row switching to Open / Unmount, **Unmount**). NOT checked: the new device popup (needs a real plug in), **Open**, **Eject** (it would power off a real disk), unplugging while mounted, udisks2 stopped at start-up. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-device-notifier-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-06-device-notifier.md`; usage in section 3.31 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Feature (panel): the pager shows a miniature of each virtual desktop with one rectangle per window (scaled from the real geometry, minimized windows hidden, windows on all desktops repeated, the active one brighter). Click switches desktop; dragging a rectangle onto another miniature moves the window there (`_NET_WM_DESKTOP`). The EWMH observer now reads each client's root rectangle (`ClientWin::rect`) and, on `ConfigureNotify`, sends a coalesced `EwmhEvent::Geometries` that only the pager redraws from. Tests: visibility rules, scaling, hit test, miniature width. NOT checked by hand: the look in the real panel and the drag between miniatures.
- Feature (ipc, desktop-config, theme-config, hub): **startup apps**. New **Startup apps** section in the Desktop tab of Settings (`StartupAppsSection`): a list of commands, each with a **Wait (s)** (0 to 600, `STARTUP_WAIT_SECS_RANGE`) to wait BEFORE launching it, Browse... for the program and Remove; **Add** appends a row. Stored as `DesktopSettings::startup_apps` (`StartupApp { command, wait_secs }`, default empty, old `desktop.json` files load unchanged; `sanitized()` trims the command, clamps the wait and drops empty commands). The wait counts from the hub start and every app has its own thread (`hub/src/startup_apps.rs`), so the waits run side by side; the command is split with `cli::tokenize` like an `exec:` hotkey and started detached in its own process group. A bad command is logged and never stops the other apps. `main.rs` starts them once, after `input_apply::start()`: saving the settings does not relaunch them. Tests: model (defaults, old files, round trip, clamping), the section values, argv splitting, a missing program, no wait, a one second wait and a failing app followed by a good one. NOT checked by hand: the section in the real Settings window and a real session start. Usage in section 3.12 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Feature (ipc, ui, desktop-config, theme-config, hub, scripts): **window transparency settings**. New **Windows** tab in Settings (`WindowsSection`) with a master switch and the opacity of the inactive and of the active windows (`DesktopSettings::transparency_enabled`, `inactive_opacity` default 88, `active_opacity` default 100, range 20 to 100, old `desktop.json` files keep the defaults). Openbox has no such option: the transparency is drawn by picom. picom reloads on `SIGUSR1` only the file it was started with, and the base `picom.conf` lives in the install prefix, so the session now starts picom on `~/.config/opusdm/picom.conf` (copied from the base when missing) and exports `OPUSDM_PICOM_BASE`; the new `hub/src/compositor.rs` rewrites the `inactive-opacity` and `active-opacity` keys of that copy from the base (libconfig forbids duplicate keys, so no `@include` override) and signals picom (`pkill -USR1`, run through `reaped::wait`), at hub start and on every settings change. No `OPUSDM_PICOM_BASE` (a session not started by OpusDM) means nothing is done; a missing picom is only logged. Updated `opusdm-session` (`build-release.sh`) and `run-x11.sh`. Tests: clamping and defaults, config rendering and writing, the section values. NOT checked by hand: the live reload under Xephyr; a running session needs a new login (or a picom restart on the user config) to follow the tab.
- Feature (panel): right click on a window in the panel tasklist opens a menu with **Move to desktop** (one entry per virtual desktop, named when `_NET_DESKTOP_NAMES` is set) and **Force kill** (`kill -9` on the `_NET_WM_PID` of the window, shown only when the client publishes it; never pid 0, 1 or the panel itself).
- Feature (ipc, ui, desktop-config, theme-config, hub): **virtual screens setting**. `DesktopSettings::virtual_screens` (default 4, range 1 to 16) is edited in the Desktop tab of Settings (new VIRTUAL SCREENS section) and applied live through `X11::set_desktop_count` (`_NET_NUMBER_OF_DESKTOPS`), also at hub start.
- Feature (input-config, hub, ipc, lister): **input settings**, a new `opusdm-input-config` binary (crate `crates/input-config`) to set the mouse (speed, flat profile, left handed, natural scrolling), the touchpad (speed, tap to click, natural scrolling, disable while typing) and the keyboard (up to 4 layouts with variants, switch key, repeat delay and rate). Every change is applied at once through `xinput set-prop`, `setxkbmap` and `xset`; **Save** writes `~/.config/opusdm/input.json`, and closing the window any other way (Cancel, Esc, the close button) applies the values that were active when it opened. The library has no GTK code: `model` (serde, `normalized()` clamps hand edited files), `store`, `parse` (xinput, setxkbmap, xset and `evdev.lst` parsers), `change` (`diff`, `all_changes`), `commands` (pure argv generation, a layout change keeps the other XKB options such as `caps:escape`), `backend` (`Backend` trait, `XBackend`, `session_problem`), `fake`, `session` (snapshot, change, commit, revert) and `apply`. A pointer with no libinput properties (Xephyr, keyboard-mouse combos) is skipped, a missing tool or touchpad greys out its tab with the reason, and a slider is debounced (30 ms) with the last value flushed before Save. The hub starts a thread (`input_apply.rs`) that applies `input.json` at start-up and again whenever the list of pointers and keyboards changes (polled every 3 s; X resets the keymap when a keyboard is plugged in); no file means the system is left alone. New internal command `OpenInputConfig` ("Input Settings", CLI `input-settings`, no parameters) and an **Input** button in the Desktop submenu of the desktop menu (`children::spawn_tool`, `sibling_bin_in`). `build-release.sh` ships the binary. The flat profile sends as many values as the device property has (two with libinput drivers before the custom profile, three after), a missing `xinput` is an error for pointer changes so the hub logs it (once per run, `apply_all` removes repeats). The tool runs its commands through `opusdm_ipc::reaped::wait` (`collect` in `backend.rs`): the hub's SIGCHLD reaper (`waitpid(-1)`) can take a child's status before a plain wait does and the wait then fails with `ECHILD`; a failed pointer listing keeps the last good signature (`next_signature`), so it never looks like a hot plug. Tests: parsers on real output, command generation, diff, `Session` (live, revert, save, failed apply, clamping), the hot plug logic, the hub tick, a child reaped by the hub and the signature. Checked by hand under Xephyr with Openbox, driven with XTEST: the Keyboard tab (add layout and repeat delay change `setxkbmap` and `xset q` at once), Cancel, Esc and the close button restore the old values, Save writes `input.json` and keeps them, the hub applies the file at start and does not reapply while the pointers are unchanged, the Mouse and Touchpad tabs are greyed out with their reason (Xephyr has no libinput devices), the Desktop > Input menu button opens the window and the menu is not cut off. NOT checked: mouse and touchpad behaviour on real hardware, hot plug with a real device, Wayland. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-input-config-design.md`; usage in section 3.29 of `docs/usage.md`.

- Fix (hub, ipc): a double click (or Open in the context menu, or a group member) on a desktop icon ignored the user file types and always started the default application: only the Lister consulted `filetypes.json`. The desktop now asks `filetypes::dblclick_for(path)` and, when the type has a double click action, sends the new `HubCmd::RunFunctionOnPaths { id, paths }`, which runs the function or inline command with that file as the selection and its folder as working directory, with no Lister involved (`Hub::run_on_paths`; `run_function_by_id` shares `steps_for` and `spawn_run`). Files with no matching type open as before. Tests: `dblclick_in` (match, no match, folder, missing file) and a hub run on explicit paths.
- Feature (ui): the drop-downs of the Function selector and of the Internal commands have a search field at the top that filters the entries (`enable_filter`, GTK `enable-search`).
- Feature (ipc, ui): the Function / Bash / Exec selector (hotkeys, user menu, script events, button banks, file types) has a fourth kind, **Internal**: a drop-down of every internal command plus an arguments box (hidden for commands with no parameters). It is stored as `internal:<Command> <arguments>` (`FuncRef::Internal`, same syntax as the command line, checked by `cli::parse`), so old files load unchanged, and it runs as that single internal step. `problem` reports an unknown command, bad arguments or a missing required parameter on the row. Tests cover parse / format, the steps, the validation and the picker draft.
- Feature (scripts): `scripts/live-update.sh` builds in release mode (through `build-release.sh`) and replaces the installed OpusDM of the running session. By default it swaps the binaries in `/opt/opusdm/bin` (with `sudo`, through a rename so running executables do not fail with "Text file busy") and restarts only `opusdm-hub`; `--config` also updates `config/` and `opusdm-session` and runs `openbox --reconfigure`; `--all` terminates every OpusDM process of the user and starts the hub again; `--prefix DIR` changes the install prefix. Openbox and picom are never restarted.
- Feature (ipc, ui, hub, lister): **virtual desktop switching**. Two new internal commands, `DesktopPrev` ("Previous Desktop", CLI `desktop-prev`) and `DesktopNext` ("Next Desktop", CLI `desktop-next`), usable in any function and in the function editor. The hub runs them with the new `X11::switch_desktop(delta)` (`opusdm-ui`): it reads `_NET_CURRENT_DESKTOP` and `_NET_NUMBER_OF_DESKTOPS` and sends the EWMH `_NET_CURRENT_DESKTOP` message to the root window; the switch wraps from the last desktop to the first and back (`wrapped_desktop`, unit tested). A window manager that reports no desktops is left alone, and an X error is logged. On first start (no `hotkeys.json`) the hub writes two global hotkeys, **Alt+1** (previous desktop) and **Alt+2** (next desktop), and adds the saved functions they run (`builtin-desktop-prev`, `builtin-desktop-next`) to `functions.json` (`bindings::seed_default_hotkeys`, `_in` for tests). An existing `hotkeys.json` is never touched, so users who already have one can bind the new commands by hand, and a hotkey the user deletes does not come back. Global hotkeys are X11 only, as before. Tests: wrapping, labels and schema, CLI names, the events a step emits, and the seed rules.

## 2026-10-05

- Feature (viewer): the **.** key switches picture scaling between smooth (default) and sharp pixels, for pixel art and detail at high zoom. `gtk::Picture` always interpolates a plain texture and ignores `image-rendering`, so the sharp mode wraps the texture in `nearest::NearestPaintable`, a small `gdk::Paintable` that snapshots it with `gsk::ScalingFilter::Nearest`. The info bar shows `pixels` while it is on and the choice survives file changes. Checked under Xephyr with a 4x4 checkerboard at 1455%: smooth is blurred, sharp has crisp squares. Test: key mapping.
- Feature (player): the **Q** key closes the player, as in the viewer.
- Refactor (media, media-ui, player, viewer): the player and the viewer share code. `timefmt` moved from the player to `opusdm-media`; the control bar (`Controls`, `Handlers`, cairo `icons`) moved to the new library crate `crates/media-ui` (`opusdm-media-ui`). `Handlers::looping` is now an `Option`: the Loop button exists only when it is set (the player sets it, so its bar is unchanged). The viewer drops its own copy of `timefmt` and its small bar and uses the shared one, so videos in the viewer now have volume and mute too (`Up` / `Down` and `m`, video only). Tests: the moved `timefmt` and icon tests, and the new key mappings.
- Feature (viewer, ipc): **image and video viewer**, a new `opusdm-viewer` binary (crate `crates/viewer`). `opusdm-viewer <file>` shows a picture or video and builds a gallery from the folder (natural order, hidden files left out). Pictures: wheel and `+` / `-` zoom, drag pan, `0` fit, `1` 100%, `r` / `R` rotate. `Left` / `Right`, `Space`, `n`, `p` move through the gallery; on a video `Space` plays / pauses and `Left` / `Right` seek 5 s (the engine is `opusdm-media`). Also a thumbnail strip (worker thread), info bar, full screen, slideshow (`s`, 4 s) and `Del` (trash, then a second question for a permanent delete). Load errors show in the window, not in a requester. Pure modules (`gallery`, `zoom`, `keys`, `slideshow`, `delete`, `kind`, `timefmt`, `args`) and the pixbuf `loader` are unit tested; the window was checked under Xephyr (fit, thumbnails, damaged file) but the key handling, wheel, drag, video and delete dialogs were not driven by hand. `default_filetypes` gains **Image file** (`exec:opusdm-viewer {f}`; existing `filetypes.json` files are not touched). `build-release.sh` ships the binary. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-viewer-gallery-design.md`; usage in section 3.28 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Feature (hub): the desktop background pauses while full screen windows hide every monitor. The video stops playing and the rotation timer stops, keeping the time the entry had left, and both go on when the desktop shows again. `fullscreen.rs` reads the window manager's EWMH state once a second (`fullscreen_windows`: windows with `_NET_WM_STATE_FULLSCREEN`, not minimized, on the current virtual desktop or all of them) and `covers_all` (unit tested) says whether each monitor is at least 90% covered by one of them, so a full screen window on one monitor of two does not pause the other. With `OPUSDM_DEBUG=1` the hub prints `desktop background play: ACTIVE` or `SUSPENDED (hidden by full screen windows)` at every change and each time an entry is shown. Checked by hand under Xephyr with openbox and `xterm -fullscreen`.
- Feature (ipc, hub, desktop-config, theme-config): **multiple desktop backgrounds**. The desktop shows a list of pictures, videos, flat colours and gradients in turn, with a cross-fade, instead of a single background. `Background` (`opusdm-ipc`) is now `entries: Vec<BackgroundEntry>` (`solid`, `gradient`, `image`, `video`, tagged by `kind`) plus the global `interval_secs` (5 to 86400, default 300), `fade_ms` (0 to 10000, default 1000) and `shuffle`; the old flat shape (`mode`, `solid`, `image_path`, ...) still loads and becomes a list of one entry. `background_css` now renders one entry, and `is_background_video` / `is_background_media` / `BACKGROUND_VIDEO_EXTS` name the video files. In the hub, `bg_sequence::Sequence` (pure, unit tested) gives the order (list or shuffled, never repeating the entry that just played, skipping unusable ones) and `background.rs` is a controller: each desktop window owns a `gtk::Stack` with a cross-fade and one page per entry, colour, gradient and picture pages are styled by a per-entry CSS class (`desktop_rule_css` now takes the entries), and a video page is a `gtk::Picture` fed by an `opusdm_media::Engine` (silent, looping) shared by every monitor. A video shorter than the interval loops until it ends and a longer one is cut; the previous video plays on until the fade is over. A file that vanished or a video that fails is skipped with a log line, and with nothing usable the default colour shows. A settings change identical to the current one does not restart the rotation, and the fade never exceeds half the interval. Settings has a new **Desktop Background** tab (`desktop-config/src/background_ui.rs`, replacing the old Background section of the Desktop tab) with the list (thumbnail or colour editors, fit, move up / down, remove), Add file... (several files, pictures and videos), Add color, Add gradient, Add from theme..., Interval, Fade and Shuffle. `SetBackground` (function step and the Lister context menu) accepts videos too and replaces the whole list with that one entry, keeping the timing. Checked by hand under Xephyr with an image, a 2 s video and a gradient (interval 5 s). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-multi-background-design.md`; usage in sections 3.12 and 3.20 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Fix (hub): the desktop context menu could run off the bottom of the screen when the Desktop submenu was opened. The menu was clamped with a fixed height and the expander made it taller afterwards. `popup_at` now uses the menu's real natural size, and `keep_menu_on_screen` re-clamps it every time the Desktop expander opens or closes. The measured size excludes the margins (`content_size`), because the margins hold the click position and GTK4 counts them in the natural size.
- Change (lister): an exec entry in the Open With menu shows its command line with no `exec:` prefix (`Open With: opusdm-player --loop {f}`). Bash entries keep their `bash:` label, and the user menu editor and the other lists are unchanged (`filetypes_ui::menu_label`).
- Feature (ipc, hub): **default file types**. On start-up the hub writes `~/.config/opusdm/filetypes.json` with two types when the file does not exist: *Audio file* (mp3, flac, ogg, oga, opus, wav, m4a, aac, wma, mka) and *Video file* (mp4, mkv, webm, avi, mov, m4v, mpg, mpeg, wmv, flv, ogv). Double click runs `exec:opusdm-player {f}` and Open With offers `exec:opusdm-player --loop {f}`, so no saved Function is needed. `filetypes::default_filetypes` holds the list and `seed_default_filetypes` (`_at` for tests) writes it only when the file is missing: an existing file, even an empty or malformed one, is never touched, and a type the user deletes does not come back. A write failure is logged and does not stop the hub. Archives and text are left out (a double click on an archive browses it before the file types are asked, and there is no View function a type can call yet); images will use `opusdm-show` later. Tests cover the validity of the defaults, the matching and the seed rules. `docs/usage.md` sections 3.10h and 3.27 updated.
- Feature (media, player, scripts): **Media player**, a new `opusdm-player` binary (crate `crates/player`) on a reusable playback library, `opusdm-media` (crate `crates/media`). `opusdm-player [--loop] <file or URL>` plays one audio or video file: play / pause, a seek slider (key frame while dragging, exact on release), current and total time, volume, mute, loop, full screen (double click or F, the bar returns when the pointer moves), and Space / arrows / M / F keys. Audio only files show the cover, title, artist and album. Buttons use cairo icons (`icons.rs`). An error (missing codec, damaged or missing file) is shown once in a requester and the exit status is 1. `opusdm-media` wraps a GStreamer `playbin3` with `gtk4paintablesink`: `Engine` (open, play, pause, seek, volume, mute, loop, `paintable()`), `Event`, `MediaError` (it names the package to install), `TagInfo`; it has no window and no OpusDM dependency, so the later desktop video background can use it (`EngineOptions { audio: false, looping: true }`). Unit tests cover the time and slider maths, arguments, view choice, URIs, tags, errors and icons; an engine test plays a generated WAV with `VideoSink::Discard` (open, duration, seek, play to the end, loop, a bad and a missing file). The windows were checked by hand under Xephyr with a video, an MP3 with a cover and a corrupt file. New system packages: `libgstreamer1.0-dev`, `libgstreamer-plugins-base1.0-dev` (build), `gstreamer1.0-gtk4`, `gstreamer1.0-plugins-good`, `gstreamer1.0-plugins-bad`, `gstreamer1.0-libav` (run). `build-release.sh` ships the binary. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-player-design.md`; usage in section 3.27 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Change (shot): the tool bar buttons show icons instead of text: a pointer for Select, a diagonal line for Line, an arrow for Arrow, a T for Text, a stroke as thick as the size for S, M and L, and a curved arrow for Undo and Redo (the tooltips show the shortcuts). They are drawn with cairo (`crates/shot/src/icons.rs`), so they do not depend on the icon theme and take the text colour of the theme. The names stay as tooltips.
- Feature (shot, scripts): **Screenshot tool**, a new `opusdm-shot` binary (crate `crates/shot`). A hotkey bound to `exec:opusdm-shot` captures the primary monitor with `GetImage` (`capture.rs`), shows it frozen and dimmed in a full-screen overlay where the user drags the area to keep (`overlay.rs`, `selection.rs`), then opens an editor (`editor.rs`) with Select, Line, Arrow (Shift locks 15 degrees) and Text tools, a 6 colour palette, three sizes, undo and redo. Annotations stay objects (`annotations.rs`) and are drawn with cairo by the same code for the canvas and the export (`render.rs`). Output: Save as... with a format bar (PNG, JPG, WebP with quality, WebP lossless; `export.rs`), Quick save (PNG in `~/Pictures/Screenshots`, never overwrites) and Copy (the window stays open so the clipboard keeps the image). New dependencies: `image` (png, jpeg) and `webp` (libwebp, lossy WebP). The model, geometry, rendering and export are unit tested; the overlay and editor were checked by hand under Xephyr. `build-release.sh` ships the binary. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-screenshot-design.md`; usage in section 3.26 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Fix (hub): a hotkey on `exec:opusdm-run` (or any OpusDM binary) was reported as `exec: 'opusdm-run' was not found` when OpusDM was not on `PATH`, which greyed out Save in Edit Hotkeys, and the hub could not start it either. The hub now puts the directory of its own executable first on `PATH` at start-up (`children::add_own_dir_to_path`, `path_with_dir`; an empty `PATH` becomes just that directory, never a trailing empty entry that means the current directory). The Listers, editors and commands it starts inherit it.
- Feature (runner, scripts): **Run window**, a new `opusdm-run` binary (crate `crates/runner`). A hotkey bound to `exec:opusdm-run` opens a small window at the top of the primary monitor, right under the panel (or at the top edge when the panel is at the bottom), with an input and a suggestion list: *Applications* (the apps of `menu.json`, else every installed visible desktop entry) and *Commands* (executables on `PATH`), ranked by exact name, name prefix, word prefix, substring, then generic name, keywords and program. Enter runs the selected row (apps through `opusdm_dock::apps::launch`) or, with no suggestion, the typed text split like an `exec:` hotkey and started detached in its own process group; a failure is shown under the input. Esc, losing the focus or a second launch closes it. Logic in `search.rs`, `sources.rs` and `launch.rs` is unit tested; `ui.rs` is the GTK window. `build-release.sh` ships the binary and `opusdm-session` and `run-x11.sh` put the binaries on `PATH`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-run-dialog-design.md`; usage in section 3.25 of `docs/usage.md`.
- Change (lister, hub): Edit Hotkeys closes its window after a successful Save, so a save is visible. Debug logs trace the save in the editor (`Edit Hotkeys: saving N hotkeys`) and in the hub (`SetBindings from conn N`), and a hub test covers `SetBindings` from an unregistered tool connection.
- Feature (ui, lister, buttonbank, hub, ipc): phase 2 of inline commands. The user menu, script events, button banks and file type actions (double click, drop, Open With) can run a Bash command or an Exec program as well as a saved Function, with the same picker as Edit Hotkeys. The picker moved to `opusdm_ui::RefPicker` (shared with the button bank crate) and gained a "none" kind for optional slots (`none_label`) and `set_reference` (refills it from the model without reporting a change); `binding_ui.rs` is gone. `funcref::title` names a reference in lists (the user menu rows, Open With labels). `bank_windows::function_usable` keeps a button with an inline command enabled (a missing saved function still greys it out), `filetypes_ui::menu_entries` keeps runnable inline commands, and "Add Menu Entry", "Add Binding" and the menu editor's "Add" work with no saved function. The script events editor repaints errors in place while you type, like Edit Hotkeys. The Shutdown hook, output window and `"$@"` rules of phase 1 apply to all of them.
- Feature (ipc, hub, lister): a hotkey can run a **Bash** command or an **Exec** program as well as a saved Function. The choice is stored in the same `function` field with a prefix (`bash:`, `bash+out:`, `exec:`), so the JSON schema and the protocol are unchanged and old files load as before. New `opusdm_ipc::funcref` (parse, format, quote, `problem`, `to_steps`) turns an inline reference into one synthetic `Run` step, which the hub already executes (no `shlex`: validation splits with the same `cli::tokenize` the Run step uses); `run_function_by_id` resolves it, so global hotkeys work with no Lister open (the command starts in `$HOME`). The validators and `prune` accept runnable inline commands and report the rest on the row (`HotkeyInvalidCommand`, `MenuInvalidCommand`, `ScriptInvalidCommand`). Edit Hotkeys has a Function / Bash / Exec picker (`binding_ui::reference_picker`, with Browse...) and repaints errors in place while you type (`revalidate`); Find Key reports `Runs the command "..."`. The user menu, script events, button banks and file types get the same picker in phase 2.
- Fix (hub, ipc), found in the review of the above: an inline bash command gets the SOURCE selection as `"$@"` (`bash -c <script> opusdm {f}`) and its script is never placeholder-expanded (`Run` arg `script`), because pasting file names into a script was shell injection and an empty `{o}` or a `${f}` silently changed it. The output window of a hub-started run (global hotkey, script event) now opens on the SOURCE Lister, and a `Shutdown` script hook can be an inline command (`to_steps_with(.., wait)`).
- Docs: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-inline-commands-design.md` specifies bash and exec inline commands wherever a Function can be referenced (hotkeys first). Nothing is implemented.
- Feature (scripts): `scripts/build-release.sh` builds the workspace in release mode and stages the deploy files in `dist/` (git-ignored): `bin/` with the four binaries, `config/` (openbox-rc.xml, picom.conf), `themes/` zips, an `opusdm-session` X11 session script, an `opusdm.desktop` template and `install.sh` (copies dist to a prefix, default `/opt/opusdm`, and registers the xsession).

- Feature (hub, lister): the Hotkey Editor is now a global window like Manage Functions. The Desktop submenu of the desktop context menu has "Edit Hotkeys" right before "Manage Functions"; it launches `opusdm-lister --edit-hotkeys` (`hotkey_editor::open_standalone`), which connects to the hub as a tool client (no Lister registration), reads hotkeys and functions from disk and sends `SetBindings` on Save. The palette's "Edit Hotkeys..." launches the same process (`launch_editor`, built on the new `function_editor::launch_tool`), so the editor no longer lives over a Lister.
- Docs: `docs/WAYLAND-PORT.md` records the Wayland porting exploration (X11 code inventory, what does not port, compositor choice, strategy, estimates). Nothing is implemented.
- Feature (sandbox): `run-x11.sh` starts a compositor (`picom` by default) inside Xephyr, with the new `picom.conf` (XRender backend, the GLX one made GTK4 lose its Vulkan device in Xephyr, shadows, inactive-window transparency, fading). Override with `XEPHYR_COMPOSITOR` (empty disables it) and `XEPHYR_PICOM_CONFIG`; if the compositor is missing the script skips it. Menus and popups get no picom shadow (it was a rectangle that ignored their rounded corners).
- Fix (lister): path bar look-ahead, `Tab` on a suggestion now always stays in the bar and moves the cursor to the end. The suggestion is detected from the selected tail text instead of a length counter that `changed` could reset.
- Feature (lister): look-ahead in the path bar. While you type an absolute local path, the folder that completes the last component is appended as selected text (`/r` shows `/ramdisk/`). `Tab` accepts it, typing on replaces it, `Backspace` removes it and `Enter` ignores it. Hidden folders are suggested only after a leading `.`; bookmarks, remote URLs and archive paths are not completed. Logic in `lister/src/pathcomplete.rs`, wiring in `build.rs` (`wire_path_completion`).
- Fix (hub, ipc): `External` function steps had the same SIGCHLD reaper race as Compress (`child.wait()` could fail with `ECHILD` and report "wait failed" for a command that ran). `poll_exit` moved from `fsops::compress` to `opusdm_ipc::reaped`, with a blocking `reaped::wait`; the External step and Compress both use it.
- Fix (fsops, hub, ipc): Compress failed with "No child processes (os error 10)". The hub's SIGCHLD reaper calls `waitpid(-1)` and collected the compressor before the job could wait for it. The reaper now records every status in `opusdm_ipc::reaped` (last 256), and `compress::poll_exit` recovers the status from there when its own wait gets `ECHILD` (it fails after 2 s without one). Compress also logs to the console with the `[compress:pid]` prefix: the command line and folder, the child pid, its exit status, its stderr and a cancel. The same race can still hit `External` function steps (`hub/src/functions.rs`, `child.wait()`).
- Feature (lister, fsops, ipc, hub): **Compress...** in the file context menu and the Command Palette. A requester asks for the archive name (editable) and the format in a drop-down listing only what the installed tools can write: `.zip`, `.tar.bz2`, `.tar.gz`, `.7z`, `.tar.xz`, `.tar.zst`, and `.gz` / `.bz2` only when one regular file is selected (`CompressFormat::available`, checked against `PATH`). Changing the format swaps the suffix in the name. It acts on the whole selection when it holds the clicked row. New `Op::Compress { archive }` (format from the name suffix) runs as a hub job: `fsops::compress::run_compress` runs `zip` / `tar` / `7z` / `gzip` / `bzip2` in the sources' folder, reports progress from the archive size, kills the tool on cancel, never overwrites an existing archive and removes a partial one. The archive goes to the DEST Lister, or to the current folder when there is none (`link_dialogs::dest_or_cwd`). Sources must share one folder, and a folder cannot be archived into itself.

## 2026-10-03

- Change (hub, lister): a Test from Manage Functions no longer starts in `$HOME` when no Lister is SOURCE. The hub answers `FunctionFailed` with `NO_SOURCE_ERROR` ("There is no SOURCE Lister: select a Lister as SOURCE, then try again."), and the Manage Functions window shows every Test failure in an OpusDM requester as well as in the log.
- Feature (lister, hub): "Manage Functions" is an independent window. It runs as its own process (`opusdm-lister --manage-functions`, `function_editor::open_standalone`; a second launch raises the open window) that links to the hub without registering as a Lister (`Client::connect_tool`), loads the list from `functions.json` and saves with `ToHub::SetFunctions`. The Command Palette entry launches it (`function_editor::launch_manager`), and the Desktop submenu of the desktop context menu has "Manage Functions" right after "Rearrange icons" (`launch_functions_manager`, sharing `spawn_lister_tool` with "Edit File Types"). The Test button shows its output, confirm prompts and copy conflicts in that window; the hub runs a `RunFunction` from such a non-Lister connection with the current SOURCE Lister as SOURCE (`Hub::run_source_for`). The now unused `Ui::function_log` is gone.
- Feature (buttonbank): the bank editor has a **Visible on all desktops** check button. It is saved as `all_desktops` in the bank file (default off, older files still load). When on, the bank window is put on every virtual desktop once mapped (`x11::set_all_desktops`, using `X11::stick_to_all_desktops`); toggling it on an open bank applies at once, and turning it off moves the window to the current desktop only (new `X11::unstick_to_current_desktop`). Verified in the Xephyr sandbox: a bank with the option on stays viewable after `wmctrl -s 2`.
- Fix (hub, panel, dock): the panel bar, the dock and the desktop windows disappeared on every virtual desktop but the first, because openbox put them on the desktop current at map time. New `X11::stick_to_all_desktops` (opusdm-ui) sets `_NET_WM_DESKTOP` to all desktops (`0xFFFFFFFF`) as a property and as an EWMH client message, and sends `_NET_WM_STATE_STICKY`; the panel, dock and desktop windows call it when realized and mapped, and list STICKY in their initial `_NET_WM_STATE`. Verified in the Xephyr sandbox: after `wmctrl -s 2` all three stay viewable.

## 2026-10-02

- Fix (lister): in the Name column only the text of a directory / file name was painted with the row colour; the icon and the space after the name showed the list ground (`file_bg`), so directories looked striped on a light theme. The `row-dir` / `row-file` / `cell-selected` classes now go on the whole cell (the icon+label box in the Name column) and the label takes the text colour through `.row-dir > label` and friends. The Name label also fills the cell, so right-click and drag work on its whole width.
- Fix (ui): the Lister file list showed white rows with the directory / file colour only behind each name in Light mode. The new `listview > row` rule in `chrome_css` (meant for drop-down popups) also matched the Lister's `columnview > listview > row` and covered the `file_bg` ground; the listview rules now apply only inside a `popover`.
- Fix (ui): in Light mode the drop-down popups and the radio / check indicators kept the dark Adwaita colours. `chrome_css` only styled `list` (ListBox), but a `gtk::DropDown` popup is a `listview`, and the indicator is a `checkbutton > radio` / `check` child node. Both now use the palette's surface, border and accent colours.
- Feature (hub, lister): the Desktop submenu of the desktop context menu has "Edit File Types" before "Edit Theme". It launches `opusdm-lister --edit-filetypes`, which opens only the File Types editor with no hub connection and no file window (`filetypes_ui::open_standalone`; `open_editor` and the standalone entry share `build_editor`, and `DialogShell::standalone` makes a parentless dialog owned by the application).
- Fix (panel): the SNI tray pinned the hub at ~300% CPU (and dbus-daemon / xdg-desktop-portal with it) after any bus activity. `watch_item` woke on every unfiltered `NameOwnerChanged` and `fetch_item` opened a new session connection each time, which emitted another `NameOwnerChanged`: a feedback loop. Now one shared session connection (`session_conn`), the name watch is filtered to the item's own name (`receive_name_owner_changed_with_args`) and `PropertiesChanged` runs on its own thread (before, it was only read by accident, thanks to the storm).
- Feature (lister): the Find dialog has an **Include binary files (slower)** check button. Off (default) keeps the old behaviour: a file with a NUL in its first 8 KiB is skipped after reading only that head. On, `FindQuery.include_binary` makes `read_for_search` and `scan_text` skip that sniff, so PDFs and other binaries are searched as lossy text (up to 16 MiB each).
- Fix (lister): the Find / Search in Files dialog did nothing when Search, Stop, View or Enter were used. Every handler held only a weak reference to the `Dialog` and nothing owned it once `find_dialog::open` returned, so `upgrade()` always failed. The window's close handler now holds the strong reference, tying the Dialog's life to the window.
- Feature (hub, theme-config): the desktop context menu has "Edit Theme" before "Settings" in the Desktop submenu. It launches `opusdm-settings --edit-theme`, which opens only the Theme Editor, without the Settings window (`build_editor_only`; `editor::open` takes the application and an optional parent; the flag is read in `main`, arguments are not passed to GApplication).
- Fix (lister): the SOURCE / DEST stat bar text now uses the theme's text colour. `chrome_css` sets `label { color }` directly, which beat the colour inherited from `.stat-source` / `.stat-dest`; the rules now also target `> label`.
- Fix (ui): DropDown (Select) labels no longer sit on a darker patch. `chrome_css` only reset `button > *`, so the nested `stack` inside `dropdown > button > box` still got the window colour; all boxes/grids/stacks/frames inside a `button` or `dropdown` are now transparent.
- Fix (lister): Command Palette arrow-key navigation scrolls the selected row into view (`select_and_reveal`). Focus stays in the search entry, so the ListBox never scrolled on its own and the selection could move off-screen.
- Fix (ui): `chrome_css` targeted ListBoxes with `list-box`, which is not a GTK4 CSS node (it is `list`), so every ListBox row kept the theme colour while its inner box got the window colour - a two-tone row (very visible in the Command Palette). Rules now use `list` / `list > row`; a selected row keeps the surface colour with a 1px blue (`#3584e4`) inset border, and the Command Palette rows are more compact (no row padding, 3px vertical margins).
- Refactor (hub): the `HUB_REQUESTER = u64::MAX` sentinel is gone. `JobMeta.requester`, `function_requesters`, `PendingFunctionRun.requester_cid`, `Hub::submit_job`, `start_function_run`, `function_failed` and `JobEngine::submit` take an `Option` (`None` = the hub itself), so every routing site must handle the no-Lister case to compile.
- Refactor (ui, panel, dock, hub): the `[80, 400, 1000]` re-placement delays live in `opusdm_ui::PLACEMENT_RETRY_MS`, and `opusdm_ui::retry_after_map(delays, f)` schedules `f` after each delay; the dock, the panel (map and re-dock) and the hub (panel strut settle, resolution change settle) use it instead of five hand-written timeout loops.
- Refactor (lister): `read_dir_rows` extracts the mtime / uid / gid / mode / size fields once, in `row_fields(&Metadata)`, instead of three copies (live symlink, broken link, plain entry).
- Refactor (logging): the remaining 40 non-test `eprintln!` calls (hub, lister, panel, dock, ui, ipc settings and themes, theme-config) now use `err_log!`, so they carry the same `[tag:pid]` prefix as the rest. `err_log!` takes an expression as its tag (`settings::read_json` passes its `app_tag`) and can be used in expression position. Test-only `eprintln!` (skipped-test notes) stay.
- Perf (lister): an auto-refresh of the folder already on screen no longer replaces the whole list. `apply_navigation` keeps the store in name order and `replace_changed_rows` splices only the span that differs (`model::changed_range`), so unchanged rows are not rebound, the scroll position stays and the keyboard cursor keeps its row (clamped to the new length). Opening another folder still replaces everything and resets the cursor. `Row` derives `PartialEq`.
- Fix (iconview): `IconEntry` carries `modified` (whole seconds since the epoch). `refresh_entries` keeps a cached thumbnail only when the entry's path and `modified` are unchanged (`thumbnail_still_valid`), so an overwritten image gets a new thumbnail on the next refresh instead of keeping the old one until the folder is reopened. The cache is still re-keyed by index; keying it by `(path, mtime)` outright was left for later.
- Refactor (hub): Function step dispatch is one exhaustive `match` on `InternalCmd` in `run_steps_cancellable`; the seven `unreachable!` arms of `run_internal` are gone. `run_internal` is split into `run_rename`, `run_make_entry` and `run_file_job` (with a small `FileJob` enum), so adding an `InternalCmd` no longer compiles until it is dispatched.
- Refactor (hub): `Hub::submit_job(requester, spec, waiter)` is the single place that submits to the job engine and records the `JobMeta`; `lister_run_job`, `HubCmd::RunJob` and `function_job` use it instead of three copies.
- Refactor (hub): one `broadcast(conns, &msg)` / `Hub::broadcast(msg)` replaces the nine copies of the "send to every registered Lister" loop (settings, bindings, formats, functions, invalidate, free caches, hidden, shutdown, job done), and `roles_msg(roles)` builds the `RoleChanged` message once for the broadcast and the reject paths of `apply_role`. (Wrapping `Bindings` / `FormatStore` in `Arc` was left out: it changes the wire types.)
- Refactor (lister): three more groups leave the `Ui` struct, like `ViewState` / `StatusBar` / `NavHistory` before them: `FormatState` (`format_state.current`, `format_state.temp`), `Caches` (`caches.dirs`, `caches.sizes`, `Caches::clear`) and `Typeahead` (`typeahead.buffer`, `typeahead.timer`). `Ui` loses six fields; no behaviour change.
- Refactor (lister): `build::build_window` (~630 lines) is split into `build_stat_bar`, `build_file_view`, `wire_filter_bar`, `build_terminal_pane`, `build_message_row`, `build_nav_bar`, `start_watcher`, `assemble_ui` and the `wire_*` functions (`wire_stat_bar`, `wire_navigation`, `wire_selection`, `wire_window_signals`, `wire_keys`, `wire_icon_view`). `build_window` itself is a ~70-line sequence of those steps. No behaviour change; smoke tested by running the hub with its first Lister, panel and dock in Xephyr.
- Perf (panel): the system tray thread no longer wakes up 60 times a second. It sleeps in `poll(2)` on the X connection and an eventfd; the new `systray::TraySender` (returned by `systray::spawn`, used by the tray widget) writes the eventfd after queueing a `TrayCmd`. An idle desktop wakes the thread zero times.
- Refactor (X11): one lazily opened connection per process for window manager requests. New `opusdm_ui::with_x11` / `X11` (atoms interned once, `pin_geometry`, `configure`, `move_resize`, `add_wm_states`, `raise_above`, `send_to_root`); it waits for a round trip when the closure returns and reconnects after an error. The dock, the hub desktop window, the panel (window hints, raise, click actions) and the button banks use it instead of ~20 `x11rb::connect` calls, so a window map no longer opens several connections.
